**Verdict: STT_6_u finds no new bug class.** Rates match or are lower (E2 2.0% vs 2.8%, E8 1.1% vs 1.7% of tests), and every u hit falls in a class the STT_6 corpus also produces, except one hit whose *shape* only the new model generates: E8 br_x `inst-038175` (not_taken). Its program stores to word A at `pc0` (last committed), opens the window at `pc1`, re-loads A at `pc2` inside the window, and feeds that value to the transmitter branch `pc3`. The trace shows the speculative load `sn:561` (0x401c3a) logging `STL_CHECK isTainted=0 isSpeculative=1` and then `Forwarding from store idx 262` at tick 2626000, ready at 2627000; the dependent `jb` (0x401c55, `sn:572`) squashes mispredicted at 2675500 while the window branch 0x401c35 is held until 5124000. That is R-SA-18 (NDA forward skips the delay) with a branch transmitter instead of a load. The shape (committed store, then an in-window re-load of the same operand before the transmitter) occurs in 1/287 u br_x tests and 0/571 STT_6_fv2 br_x tests at 2%, so it is the instance-based protection admitting a store-then-reload shape that STT_6's location-based set drops. It is not a new bug. No other u-only class was found. Not checked: E13 UV4/CS-1 attribution of the u hits (no kept traces); the E2 "ready 3 cycles after visible" class is shared and belongs to queue row 19.

- Workspace: `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108` (`PROVENANCE_sha256.txt` lists every `.als`/script).

  - Exact sizes k = 1..4 run as one partition each.
  - k = 5 and 6 are also split by transmitter kind: one partition per kind in the leakage function, plus a `rest` partition that must be UNSAT, as a coverage check.

  - SPT v2's partitions k1 + k2 + k3 = 0 + 0 + 546, exactly the 546 resolve-hooks found at `but 3`, so the partitioning loses and duplicates nothing.

  - `STT_6_stl_core` must total 306 (the Mac's exhaustive count).
  - Every `rest` partition must be UNSAT.
  - The old RO+RI cores (2 each for STT/Recon) must appear among the RI-only cores (RO+RI-minimal implies RI-minimal).
  - A sample of cores must concretize through parsexml.
  - Transmitter-kind mix should be compared with `testsets_latest`.
- **Watch:** the cafe2 Slurm holder job (`hold_simspect`, 18374614, 2-day limit) started 2026-11-08 ~00:49Z and expires ~00:49Z 2026-11-10 (squeue, 2026-11-09 05:00Z). Renew it (`make hold`) before it expires, so ssh access to cafe2 continues; the container itself runs under docker, not the job.

**Stage 1.5: RUNNING since 2026-11-09 01:54Z** (tmux `alloy_stage15_1108`; `launch_stage15.py`; status `status/stage15_status.json`; results `s15/<family>/results.jsonl`).
- **Method:** each core is pinned into its production model with `stage_seed.py` and fleshed out at exactly 6 instructions, one run per core, 24 in parallel, 30 min per core.
- **Size (Anna, 2026-11-08): about 300,000 instances per family.**
  - That is split evenly over the family's cores: per-core cap = ceil(300000 / cores), e.g. STT_6_stl 981 per core.
  - If a family has more than 300,000 cores, a uniform sample of 300,000 is run (seed 20261108) at 1 per core; this is recorded in `s15/<family>/PROVENANCE.json`.
- **Scheduling:** each family starts when its Stage 1 completes. STT_6_stl started first.
- **STT_6_stl: DONE (01:58Z-02:06Z).** All 306 cores were run at 981 per core: 266,946 instances; 266 cores CAPPED and 40 EXHAUSTED (fewer than 981 programs exist). Results are in `s15/STT_6_stl/`. The other four families are waiting for their Stage 1.
- **Sanity:**
  - One STT core alone fleshes out to more than 31,000 programs in 15 min (not exhausted), so a per-core cap is required.
  - 38/38 sampled STT_6_stl Stage-1.5 instances concretize through `/tests/simspect` parsexml (2096e32a) in `stl_bypass` mode.
- **Contention (2026-11-09 01:55Z):** load hit 63-68 of 64 cores when Stage 1.5 started. Two causes: another session's gem5 campaign (`check_ld.py --jobs 16` in `/tests/simspect_fixed_20261030`), and the JVMs' many GC/compiler threads.
  - The fix: restarted with 24 jobs and `JAVA_TOOL_OPTIONS=-XX:+UseSerialGC -XX:CICompilerCount=2`; load is back to about 55.
  - Using cafe1/cafe3 would add 128 cores (setup below).
- **Next after Stage 1.5:** merge each family (dedup across cores with `STAGE2_compilation/find_duplicates.py`, then renumber into `testsets/<family>__twostage/xml`). Then Stage 2, after the vocabulary expansion.

**Using cafe1 / cafe3** (both have 64 cores and about 500 GB free; checked with sinfo 2026-11-09). Each needs a Slurm holder job before ssh works (pam_slurm_adopt). Use a distinct job name, because `scripts/sim` tracks `hold_simspect`:
1. `ssh cafe1 true` once, interactively, to accept cafe1's host key (it currently fails host-key verification).
2. `ssh sc "sbatch --account=cafe --nodelist=cafe3 -p cafe-interactive --mem=32G --time=2-00:00:00 --job-name=hold_simspect_cafe3 --wrap 'sleep infinity'"`, and the same with `cafe1`.
3. Then check whether docker and the `gem5_dev` image (or Java 17) exist there. Copy the workspace (`/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108` minus `out/`, `s15/`), and give that node its own share of families or partitions with its own status JSON (add it to `specs/eval_runs.json`).

## How the pieces connect

```
 Alloy model family            STAGE1_alloy/models/...                 which leaks exist (symbolic)
   Stage 1    core model       <model>_core*.als (RI-only; SPT too)    → minimal leak skeletons ("cores")
   Stage 1.5  production model <model>.als + pin one core (round 2)     → full instances grown around each core, cores in parallel
      ▼
 testset                       testsets*/<model>/xml/                   one XML per leak instance
      │  Stage 2: parsexml.py + compile_annotate.py (per branch mode, or stl_bypass for STL)
      ▼
 programs                      testsets*/<model>/<mode>/{asm,ann}/      .s + .ann.json per test
      │  Stage 3: one run config = gem5 binary + flags + hook state + checker
      ▼
 eval unit (one row below)     runconfigs/... or results/<x>/run_config.jsonc
      │  checker (check_ld / check_br / check_footprint / STL window checker)
      ▼
 results                       results/<results_name>__<timestamp>/<mode>/<kind>/
```

**How models, builds and run configs relate:**
- **One model family feeds many builds.** The STT_6 family is the stimulus for every row except ReCon (Recon_6), SPT (SPT_6_oneLP_v2) and OTB.
- **One row = one gem5 binary with one flag set,** so each row has its own run config and its own results directory. Rows that share the Kaxiras-group binary `gem5-recon-modded` (Unsafe, STT, ReCon, NDA, DoM, Doppelganger) differ only in flags, and they are still separate runs.
- **Copy the run config verbatim** when reproducing a hit. It carries the full hook state (branch force, unresolved stall, fnc stall); see CLAUDE.md.

**Harness additions, the same on every row:**
- the SimSpect hook flags and debug flags;
- the derived branch force, the resolve stall, and the execution-validity gate. These are on all 8 hooked builds; see [`../design/RESOLUTION_HOOKS.md`](../design/RESOLUTION_HOOKS.md) and [`../findings/bug/BRANCH_ANNOTATION_AND_FORCE.md`](../findings/bug/BRANCH_ANNOTATION_AND_FORCE.md).

**Where rows deviate from the authors' configuration:** everything else matches it, with one deliberate exception. The footprint rows E12-E14 use an amplified geometry: shrunk L1D, and InvisiSpec 2 TBEs / CleanupSpec 8 (Anna, 2026-11-08; the paper's discussion explains it). These shrunk configs are the default eval runs. AMuLeT's exact flags (the T rows below) are used only for timing numbers and for confirming bugs. **Confirmed by Anna (2026-11-08):** the taint-tracking rows (E1-E11: STT, ReCon, NDA, DoM, Doppelganger, SPT) deliberately keep unshrunk caches, because their leak test (did the transmitter obtain its data inside the window) does not depend on cache pressure; only the footprint rows shrink. **Known deviation found 2026-11-08:** ReCon is implemented in Ruby/SLICC and the authors run it with `--ruby` and the `ruby-singlecore` protocol folder (`documentation/docs/Files/email.txt:20-23`), but E6 runs classic `--caches`, where the reveal never takes effect (`wiki/findings/audits/2026-11-08/SOURCE_AUDIT_RECON.md` R-SA-1). Until that is resolved, E6 measures Recon-STT, not ReCon. **Resolved as far as the build goes (recon-ruby, 2026-11-08):** a correct ReCon build now exists, `/work/gem5-recon-ruby3` (MESI_Three_Level + `ruby-singlecore`, the authors' recipe); see E6 and "ReCon under the authors' setup (verified 2026-11-08)" below. Two things still block a ReCon result: one config line (the authors' `se.py` ships `from ruby import Ruby` commented out) and the testset, which cannot exercise the reveal.

**Checker and harness state on the AMuLeT builds** (deployed to `/tests/simspect_fixed_20261030/`, 2026-11-08, md5-verified, per hooks-v2):
- The `check_footprint` by_pc fix: it had wrongly disqualified 114/3000 InvisiSpec tests and 202 CleanupSpec records.
- CleanupSpec's ownership rule ("count as both").
- `gem5_common._unglue`: native debug-print residue no longer drops trace lines.
- Hooks v2 on all three footprint builds; every trace starts `META:hooks … ver=2:build=<name>`.

**Before the final batch:**
- `make sync`. The lc/fnc-from-Commit-trace fix (`gem5_common.fill_retire_from_commit`, 2026-11-07) is in this repo and the cafe2 fixed copy, but not yet in `/tests/simspect`.
- Every final run config needs its `sample_fraction` set. The templates `runconfigs/fixed_20261030/*_fixed_latest.jsonc` still say `0.02` from validation.
- Point every final run config's `testset_dir` / `results_name` at the regenerated testsets.
- **Grow the instruction vocabulary for the fanout (Stage 2) runs (Anna, 2026-11-08).** `wiki/design/VOCAB_TABLES.md` covers this, with decisions V1-V9. Everything beyond other_n is an approval-gated `parsexml.py` emitter change, so it must land before Stage 2 of the final batch, not during it.

**Naming:** this manifest says **Stage 1.5** for the pinned flesh-out that the briefs call "Stage 2 / round 2". That is the name in the pipeline figure and `URGENT_TODO.md` (decided 2026-11-02), because "Stage 2" already means concretization.

### The fanout pipeline (all bugs), 2026-11-09

**Superseded for new runs by "The fanout pipeline v3 (all bugs), 2026-11-10" below** (Anna's 2026-11-10 decisions TA-1, TA-2, TA-6). Kept as the record of how `STT_6_fv2fp_rmw` / `_rmw2` and every 2026-11-09 first-detection row were made.

The one recipe every defense runs (fanoutgaps, 2026-11-09). Steps 1-4 make ONE corpus that every defense shares; step 5 is per defense. Every knob is listed with its value and the file that sets it. Today's instance of the corpus is `STT_6_fv2fp_rmw` (cafe3 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/testsets_twostage/`); the final corpus will be regenerated from the new models (loosepin, `wiki/design/TWO_PHASE_MODELS.md`), then steps 2-5 rerun unchanged.

1. **Model (Stage 1 → Stage 1.5).** Today: two-stage STT_6 (Stage 1 core → Stage 1.5 pinned flesh-out), 296,170 instances merged and deduped to 93,041 programs by `merge_stage15.py` → `testsets_twostage/STT_6/xml` (`wiki/state/RUNNING.md`). Final: the loose-pin models, once Anna signs them off (model work is the loosepin agent's, not this recipe's).
2. **Stage 2 (concretization), both branch modes.** `STAGE1_alloy/models/proposed_twophase/stage2_sharded.py` with `runconfigs/twostage_fv2_20261109/stage2_STT_6_fv2fp.jsonc`: `branch_modes` [mispredict_not_taken, mispredict_taken]; `instruction_tables` = `STAGE2_compilation/instruction_tables/proposed/fv2_vocab.jsonc` (default table + inc + cmov_rr; `branch_forms.br_cmp: true`; `branch_forms.jmp_direct: 0.5`, i.e. each resolved, correctly-not-taken, non-transmitter branch becomes a direct jmp with p 0.5); `interleave.enabled: false`; `reg_naming` random, seed 0; `fanout_v2` enabled with `pre_window_reads: true`, `splice` (p_xmit 0.75, p_other 0.25, ops add0 or0 xor0 and_m1 imul1 lea0 not2 neg2 rol_ror add_sub xor2 bswap2), `fillers` (count 1-3, ops add xor and imul lea shl), `otb` (p 0.5); `branch_cond.from_alloy: true`; `fpr` enabled (`loads: consumed`, xmm 0-15, forms route direct route_alu direct_alu). Output `<testset>/<mode>/{llvm,asm,ann}/`.
3. **Post-pass RMW** (Stage-2 post-pass; never in parsexml): `STAGE2_compilation/make_rmw_corpus.py --select eligible --fraction 0.5 --seed 20261109 --complete --jobs 16`. Every stem goes through the gates first (ld transmitter, no Alloy rf consumer of its output, asm liveness, faithful re-resolution, regular address shift), then exactly half of the ELIGIBLE load-transmitter tests per mode become `orq $0,<mem>` (lowest sha256("20261109:<stem>") rank). The other tests are byte copies; `rmw_stems.tsv` lists the RMW tests. `--select hash` is the old rule (draw before the gates), which gave `STT_6_fv2fp_rmw`. Rebuilt with the new rule as `STT_6_fv2fp_rmw2` (row C1-fv, numbers there).
4. **Post-pass jmp** (Stage-2 post-pass, opt-in, run AFTER step 3): `STAGE2_compilation/make_jmp_corpus.py --p 0.5 --seed 20261109 --jobs 16`. With probability p per test (sha256("jmp:20261109:<stem>")), one direct `jmp` to the next instruction (`eb 00`, value-neutral) goes in its own inline-asm block right before the marker block of an Alloy pc k with first unresolved branch < k <= transmitter, never before a mispredict_taken BTB-forced target (the redirect would skip it); position = sha256("jmppos:20261109:<stem>") over the candidates. Every annotation address is re-resolved with compile_annotate.py and checked instruction by instruction (an older short jcc may grow when its target moves; that is allowed and checked). Recorded per test as `ann.jmp_insert` and in `jmp_stems.tsv`; unselected tests and p = 0 are byte copies. Why: on STT_6_fv2fp, `jmp_direct` leaves 48.7% of tests with no jmp at all and 89.4% with no jmp inside the window (between the first unresolved branch and the transmitter); with this pass at p 0.5 those become 24.1% and 44.4% (computed over all 93,041 stems per mode; the per-test checks passed on 800/800 sampled tests at p 1). Not built as a full corpus yet (the final corpus comes from the new models). Smoke results: claudelog 2026-11-09 'fanoutgaps'.
5. **Stage 3, per defense** (cafe `gem5_dev`, `TMPDIR=/tmp` always: gem5 SE copies the `--cmd` path onto the stack, so another TMPDIR length moves stack lines and changes verdicts, BUGS.md H-STK). First detection: `STAGE3_gem5/timing/timing_driver.py --seed 20261109 --xmit-kinds all` (one order for every defense: the sorted (mode, stem) pairs shuffled with `random.Random(20261109)`; every transmitter kind runs, `XMIT_KINDS` in the launchers, default all), then `classify_hits.py --defense <d> --testset <corpus>` (H-PAD exclusion: events at or past `.text` end are harness padding, `excluded_HPAD:<sig>`; SETUP exclusion for `full` records: the setup sweep's own loads, `excluded_SETUP:<sig>`). Harness additions on every row: BTB branch force (`branch_ann_enable: true`), resolve stall on the mispredicted branches (parity rows: `sweep.points [0]`, `unresolved_stall_cycles` 5000; eval rows: per-branch random stall from {2500, 5000, 10000}), `fnc_commit_stall_cycles` 0. Cache setups (`gem5.cache_setup`, `STAGE3_gem5/cache_setup.py`): `warm` (prologue zero-fill, untouched), `cold` (clflush the test frame), `random` (per-test warm/cold from sha256(seed, stem), seed `simspect-cache-random-v1`), `full` (L1D-sized load sweep so every set starts full). Each is its own run.

| defense | first-detection configs (AMuLeT sizes) | cache setups | checker + criterion | classifier, targets | eval row (shrunk) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| InvisiSpec-Futuristic | `runconfigs/amulet_parity_fv2fp_rmw/T1_*` (warm), `T1f_*` (full) | warm, full (cold and random are refused: clflush is a no-op on InvisiSpec) | `check_footprint.py`, `specs/leakage/invisispec.json` | `classify_hits --defense invisispec`: UV1, UV1_transient | E12a (Futuristic), E12b (Spectre), `runconfigs/twostage_fv2_20261109/E12{a,b}_<testset>.jsonc`, warm |
| CleanupSpec | `runconfigs/amulet_parity_fv2/T2_*` (warm), `T2c_*` (cold = AMuLeT parity), `T2r_*` (random), `runconfigs/amulet_parity_fv2fp_rmw/T2f_*` (full) | warm, cold, random, full | `check_footprint.py`, `specs/leakage/cleanupspec.json` | `classify_hits --defense cleanupspec` (UV4, UV5, CS1 via `cleanupspec_skip_evidence.py`), then `classify_cs_rmw.py --rmw-stems <corpus>/rmw_stems.tsv` (UV3 and E→M credited only to the test's own RMW; padding RMW excluded) | E13, random |
| SpecLFB | `runconfigs/amulet_parity_fv2fp_rmw/T3_*` (warm), `T3c_*` (cold), `T3r_*` (random), `T3f_*` (full) | warm, cold, random, full | `check_footprint.py`, `specs/leakage/speclfb.json` | `classify_hits --defense speclfb`: UV6, walk_order, jmp_shadow | E14, random |
| amulet-STT | `runconfigs/amulet_parity_fv2fp_rmw/T4_*` (warm) | warm | per kind: `check_ld.py` / `check_br.py` | `classify_hits --defense stt`: S1_xmm4, data-obtained gate (`disq_not_obtained`) | E4 (AMuLeT comparison only); E3 stt-master is SimSpect's STT row |
| other eval rows | none | warm | per kind (check_ld / check_br) | `bucket_hits.py` + `diag_*` | E1, E2, E3, E9 (and E6 ReCon on its own model) |

Launchers: `STAGE3_gem5/timing/firstdet_launch_rows.sh` (ROWS, N, P), `firstdet_cs_launch.sh` (CleanupSpec), `firstdet_full_launch.sh` (full), each with at most 16 workers on a shared node; eval campaigns: `runconfigs/twostage_fv2_20261109/make_campaign_configs.py` + `launch_campaigns.py` (`sample_fraction` must be set for the final batch; today 0.02). What a `br_x` (branch-transmitter) hit means on the footprint defenses: `check_footprint` does not look at the transmitter at all; a test hits when any speculative actor in the window leaves a footprint the criterion counts, so a `br_x` hit is a footprint left by the loads of that test's window (for example the speculative load feeding the branch), and its signatures (UV6, jmp_shadow, UV4, ...) mean exactly what they mean on a load test. The `br_x` tests have always been in the first-detection rows (T1 873 of 3000, T3r 873 of 3000, T2c 1166 of 4000), because the configs' `filter.xmit_kind: ld` key has never been read by anything.

### The fanout pipeline v3 (all bugs), 2026-11-10

The recipe for every new run, starting with the loose-pin regeneration (regen-lp) and its first detection (recipe-v3, 2026-11-10). It is v2 with four changes Anna decided on 2026-11-10 after the targeting audit (`URGENT_TODO.md` "DECIDED 2026-11-10"; audit `wiki/findings/audits/2026-11-10/TARGETING_AUDIT.md`): **no fanout OTB transform** (TA-1: OTB must come from the models), **jmp placed uniformly over the test body** (TA-2), **one cache policy for every defense, per-test random over all setups** (TA-6), and RMW + jmp in the corpus as vocabulary (TA-5; today still through the two post-passes, a first-class table version is the pending DECIDE VOCAB-RJ). Every knob and its value:

| step | knob | value (v3) | changed from v2? | set by |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 model | corpus | the loose-pin models (LP-1: `_lpP` ∪ `_lpA` 2-branch cap, merged and fingerprint-deduped; AMuLeT defenses on `Footprint_6_u`, taint defenses on `STT_6_u`/`Recon_6_u`; SPT unchanged) | yes (was two-stage STT_6) | regen-lp, `wiki/design/TWO_PHASE_MODELS.md` "Loose-pin regeneration (regen-lp)" |
| 2 Stage 2 | config | `runconfigs/regen_lp_20261110/stage2_fv3.jsonc` (copy of `twostage_fv2_20261109/E3_STT_6_fv2fp.jsonc`, only `fanout_v2.otb.enabled` and `results_name` differ) | yes | stage2_sharded.py |
| 2 | branch modes | mispredict_not_taken, mispredict_taken | no | config |
| 2 | instruction table | `instruction_tables/proposed/fv2_vocab.jsonc` (default + inc + cmov_rr; `branch_forms.br_cmp: true`, `jmp_direct: 0.5`) | no | config |
| 2 | fanout_v2 splice | enabled, p_xmit 0.75, p_other 0.25, ops add0 or0 xor0 and_m1 imul1 lea0 not2 neg2 rol_ror add_sub xor2 bswap2; `pre_window_reads: true` | no | config |
| 2 | fanout_v2 fillers | enabled, count 1-3, ops add xor and imul lea shl | no | config |
| 2 | **fanout_v2 otb** | **`enabled: false`** (p 0.5 left in place but unread) | **yes (TA-1)** | config |
| 2 | fpr | enabled, `loads: consumed`, xmm 0-15, forms route direct route_alu direct_alu | no | config |
| 2 | reg_naming / branch_cond / interleave | random seed 0 / `from_alloy: true` / off | no | config |
| 3 RMW post-pass | `make_rmw_corpus.py` | `--select eligible --fraction 0.5 --seed 20261109 --complete` (exactly half of the eligible load-transmitter tests become `orq $0,<mem>`) | no | command line |
| 4 jmp post-pass | `make_jmp_corpus.py` | **`--p 0.5 --seed 20261109`, default `--placement uniform`**: before the marker of a pc drawn uniformly over EVERY pc of the test body, never before a mispredict_taken BTB target; `ann.jmp_insert.{placement, in_window}` | **yes (TA-2)**; v2 placed it only between the first unresolved branch and the transmitter, now `--placement window`, logged as NOT for reported numbers | command line |
| 5 Stage 3 | **cache setup** | **`gem5.cache_setup: random_all`** on every defense: per test uniform over the setups the build allows, warm/cold/full (InvisiSpec warm/full), `sha256(seed, stem)`, seed `simspect-cache-random-v1`; per record `cache_setup`, `cache_setup_mode`, `cache_setup_seed`, `cache_setup_choices`; no best-setup reporting | **yes (TA-6)**; v2 ran warm/cold/random/full as separate rows | run config, `STAGE3_gem5/cache_setup.py` |
| 5 | first-detection configs (AMuLeT defenses, AMuLeT sizes) | `runconfigs/regen_lp_20261110/T1ra_Footprint_6_u_lp_fv3.jsonc` (InvisiSpec-Futuristic), `T2ra_…` (CleanupSpec), `T3ra_…` (SpecLFB): copies of `amulet_parity_fv2fp_rmw/T1`, `amulet_parity_fv2/T2c`, `amulet_parity_fv2fp_rmw/T3r` with only `cache_setup` and paths changed | yes | |
| 5 | order, kinds, harness | `timing_driver.py --seed 20261109 --xmit-kinds all`; `TMPDIR=/tmp`; BTB force + resolve stall as v2; `classify_hits.py` (H-PAD; SETUP exclusion now per record, applies to the full third) | no | |

**Measured effect of the jmp change** (`STAGE2_compilation/jmp_placement_share.py` on `STT_6_fv2fp_full`, 93,041 stems per mode, p 0.5, seed 20261109; output cafe3 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/recipe_v3/share/`): tests with a jmp inside the window (first unresolved branch to transmitter, counting `jmp_direct` jmps too) are **28.8% (not_taken) and 23.6% (taken) with uniform placement**, against 10.6% with no post-pass and 55.6% with the old window placement. 42.6% / 31.3% of the inserted jmps land in the window (the window's share of the test body). Tests with no jmp at all: 24.1% either way. Real build at p 1 (400 stems per mode of `STT_6_fv2fp_rmw2`, cafe3 `recipe_v3/smoke_jmp_uniform_p1/`): 800/800 written, every one at its first-drawn position, 135 + 86 in the window. One check was relaxed for this: a `jmp_direct` jump whose label sits right before the chosen block now lands on the inserted jmp (by design every entry into pc k passes through it); before, those 7/300 tests fell back to the next position.

**Smoke of `random_all`** (cafe3, 12 tests per config on `STT_6_fv2fp_rmw2`, `recipe_v3/smoke_T{1,2,3}ra/`): 36/36 `status: ok`, 0 errors; SpecLFB and CleanupSpec cold 6 / full 4 / warm 2, InvisiSpec warm 7 / full 5.

**Not changed by v3 and still to disclose** (audit S5, S7, H1; S3 FP routing is NOT a disclosure, Anna 2026-11-15): `jmp_direct`, the RMW transmitter rewrite and the cold/full setups were each added after a related bug was known; v3 removes the targeting (OTB, window jmp, best-setup reporting) but not that history.

**Corpora built with the old jmp rule:** `STT_6flat_fv2fp_rmw_jmp` (oldfanout, cafe3, created 2026-11-10 09:01 UTC; its provenance rule says "first_unresolved_pc < k <= xmit.pc") uses the v2 window placement, so its jmp share is targeted (TA-2) and it is not a v3 corpus.

## ReCon under the authors' setup (verified 2026-11-08, recon-ruby)

**Builds on cafe2 (read-only survey):**
- `/work/gem5-recon`, `/work/gem5-recon-modded` and `/work/gem5-recon-stl` all have `src/mem/ruby` identical to `ruby-versions/ruby-singlecore`, with 0 differing entries (`diff -rq`); against `ruby-default` there are 17. All three `build/X86` were built `PROTOCOL = 'MESI_Two_Level'` (`build/X86/gem5.build/variables`); the ARM builds are CHI. Before 2026-11-08 no MESI_Three_Level build existed.
- `ruby-singlecore` also adds the reveal bit to MESI_Two_Level (`MESI_Two_Level-L1cache.sm`: `Reveal` event, `revealTheBit`, `readCallback(..., revealBits)`). So the existing binaries could run a Ruby reveal under `--ruby`. The authors' instructions say MESI_Three_Level, though, and their example command uses L0 options, which exist only in `configs/ruby/MESI_Three_Level.py:64-67`. The new build follows the authors.

**New build `/work/gem5-recon-ruby3`** is a copy of `/work/gem5-recon-modded` with `build/` and `m5out*` left out. Its `src` and `configs` have 0 differences from recon-modded, so the SimSpect hooks came along, and `src/mem/ruby` has 0 differences from `ruby-singlecore`. Built with `/usr/bin/scons build/X86/gem5.opt PROTOCOL=MESI_Three_Level -j 24` (script `build_ruby3.sh`, log `build_ruby3.log`, EXIT=0, 2026-11-09 02:47 UTC). There are no source edits. The one new file is `configs/example/se_ruby.py`: the authors' `se.py` with line 56 `# from ruby import Ruby` uncommented. The authors ship that import commented out (`/work/gem5-recon/configs/example/se.py:55`), so `se.py --ruby` dies with `NameError: name 'Ruby' is not defined` (se.py:224). Their own runs used `fs.py`, which imports Ruby (`fs.py:53`). We use `gem5.opt`, not their `gem5.fast`, because the checkers need DPRINTFs.

**Legitimacy of E2 (Recon-STT, scheme 2 without `--allow_leaked`, classic caches): legitimate.**
- STT's protection is CPU-side. Tainted loads are held in the LSQ (`lsq_unit.cc:2095-2104`, `delayUnit.insertTaintedLoad`), and tainted instructions in the IQ (`inst_queue.cc:1453`). Taints are kept in `secure_scheme/taint_tracker.cc`.
- The only memory-side defense state is ReCon's reveal bit and DoM's `L1_ONLY` flag. Every reveal path is gated on `cpu->allowLeaked`: the Reveal send `lsq_unit.cc:870`; the load-pair table `:946`; the reveal untaint `taint_tracker.cc:142`; NDA's allowlist `lsq_unit.cc:199`; `inst_queue.cc:1053`; `commit.cc:1050`. `L1_ONLY` is set only for `Scheme::DoM` (`lsq.cc:1442-1446`).
- So without `--allow_leaked`, no reveal bit is ever set, and classic vs Ruby does not change what STT protects.
- Measured: on 50 Recon_6 load tests, Recon-STT gave the same 2 hits (inst-021381, inst-133326) on classic recon-modded and on Ruby ruby3, with identical cache-fate counts.
- Caveat: `L1_ONLY` is handled only by classic `BaseCache` (`mem/cache/base.cc:462`), so DoM (E9) must stay on classic caches.

**Legitimacy of the checkers and hooks under `--ruby` MESI_Three_Level: valid, with the same pre-existing gate bug as classic.**
- The hooks are CPU-side: branch force (`bpred_unit.cc`), resolve stall and wake (`iew.cc`), fnc stall (`commit.cc`). No hook touches `src/mem/ruby`.
- On every validation run (5 configs, 2 samples, 484 Ruby records), `br_resolve_source=observed` and 0 records failed the validity gate.
- `check_ld` reads only LSQUnit/O3PipeView/Commit lines, which are CPU-side. Under Ruby they are the same lines: the smoke trace has 270 `Memory request … was sent`.
- The known recon gate bug (`wiki/findings/bug/recon_cache_reach_gate/README.md`, URGENT_TODO DECIDE) applies unchanged. These builds never print `successfully sent out packet(s)`, so fates are only `store_forward` / `executed_no_packet` / `never_executed`.
- Unsafe control on Ruby: 43/50 hits (s50 sample) and 21/50 (ldld50), all `store_forward` fate.
- The AMuLeT Ruby builds use hooks v2 (Ruby cache-event lines, `wiki/design/SIMSPECT_HOOKS.md`); recon-ruby3 does not need them, because `check_ld` needs no cache-event lines.

**Does the reveal fire? It is sent and marks the right address, but it never untaints anything in our tests.**
- Stats: `system.cpu.lsq0.revealRequests` (`lsq_unit.cc:381,911`), `system.cpu.taint_tracker.allowlistedTaintsFromTT` (`taint_tracker.cc:30,144`).
- Workspace `/tests/simspect_fixed_20261030/results/recon_ruby3_val/`: `run_val*.sh`, `analyze_val.py`, `summary_{s50,ldld50,revealprobe}.json`.

| sample | config | hits | revealRequests | allowlistedTaintsFromTT |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| s50 (50 random Recon_6 ld tests) | ReCon Ruby | 2 | 0 | 0 |
| s50 | Recon-STT Ruby / classic, classic + allow_leaked | 2 / 2 / 2 | 0 | 0 |
| ldld50 (50 tests with a committed LD→LD pair) | ReCon Ruby | 0 | 71 (43 stems) | 0 |
| ldld50 | classic + allow_leaked (old E6) | 0 | 71 (43 stems) | 0 |
| ldld50 | Recon-STT Ruby / classic | 0 / 0 | 0 | 0 |
| revealprobe (inst-063358, its speculative load retargeted to the revealed slot) | ReCon Ruby / classic + allow_leaked | 0 / 0 | 1 per run | 0 |

- A ProtocolTrace of the probe on ruby3 shows the Reveal reach L0 at the right address: `3042500 Seq Begin [0x34e8, line 0x34c0] Reveal`, then `3043000 L0Cache Reveal M>M [0x34e8 …]`. 0x34e8 is offset 40 in the line, which is the committed LD1's slot. Classic sends it to paddr 0 (R-SA-1).
- It does not untaint anything, because the speculative load and the transmitter execute at tick 3040000, before LD2 retires at 3042000. A reveal sent at LD2's commit is too late in a one-pass litmus. (Those probe tests are not hits under Unsafe either: the signal falls before `lc_retire`.)
- A scan of all 150,000 Recon_6 `mispredict_not_taken` tests found 1,210 with a committed LD→LD pair and **0** where a speculative load reads the revealed slot. This matches the Alloy side, where R-1 makes the reveal a no-op.

## stt-master: upstream STT master for E3 (built 2026-11-08)

- **Source:** `https://github.com/cwfletcher/stt` @ `5bf41109477e1c9c5822674afa4103e6eb16ff09`. Fetched as a tarball (sha256 `aa2281ad…4cc1c`), plus a bare clone for the log, both in `/work/stt-master-src/`. `e3283b9..5bf4110` = `18e304f fix pending squash bug` + the merge. It changes only `src/cpu/o3/iew_impl.hh` (+13/-1, in `executeInsts`): a tainted mispredicted branch now gets `hasPendingSquash(true)` instead of squashing in IEW.
- **Hooks:** `/work/stt`'s full working-tree diff vs `e3283b9` (`/work/stt-master-src/stt_worktree_vs_e3283b9.diff`: stt.patch + resolve hooks + wake edit), applied with `patch -p1`. Every hunk applied exactly, with no offset or fuzz. `se_exp.py`, `se_futuristic.py` and `se_slowcomm.py` were copied unchanged, along with the `README -> README.md` symlink that `/work/stt` has (the SConscript needs `README`).
- **Tree check:** `diff -rq` of `/work/stt` vs `/work/stt-master` (excluding build/.git) shows only `iew_impl.hh` (the upstream fix) plus untracked `README`/`parsetab.py`.
- **Build:** `python2 scons-2.5.1 build/X86/gem5.opt -j16`, the same as `gem5-hooks/resolve/rebuild_resolve_hooks.sh` (`/work/stt-master/build_stt_master.sh`, log `build_stt_master.log`, EXIT=0). gem5.opt sha256 `ca7259637a59c3f2…5c1e`.
- **Validation** (`/tests/simspect_fixed_20261030/results/stt_master_val/`):
  - `hook_audit.py --build stt-master` vs `--build stt` (12 tests, seed 0): the check dicts are identical on all 12, including every tick. The one `resolved_correct` FAIL (inst-116576) is the same on `/work/stt`.
  - E3 sample (`testsets_sttmaster_val/STT_6_s60`, 30 ld + 30 br_x per mode, 532 records): same verdicts, 0/120 hits on both. 51 records differ in timing, all `br_x` transmitters (9 test×modes, a few cycles later). That is the expected effect of the pending-squash fix on tainted transmitter branches (`compare_e3.json`).
  - S1 xmm4 (`testsets_fpr_s1check/STT_6_fpr_xmm4`): 98/100 hits on stt-master, the same stems as `/work/stt`.
- **Interaction to decide:** the resolve-stall hook's deferred release (`iew_impl.hh`, `stallBcast` lambda) copies the *pre-fix* squash code. So a stalled (annotated) branch that is mispredicted *and* tainted still squashes from IEW instead of getting the new pending squash. The hook audit showed no difference on 12 tests. It matters only if a forced-unresolved branch's condition is tainted.

## needsTSO for E3 (Anna #14, 2026-11-08)

- **What it changes in `/work/stt`** (and in `/work/stt-master`, which has identical LSQ code):
  - Store writeback is serialized, so only one store is in flight to the cache at a time (`lsq_unit_impl.hh:1358`, `:1681`, `:1873`).
  - On an invalidation snoop that hits a load's line, the load and *all younger loads* are squashed (`lsq_unit_impl.hh:564`, via `checkSnoop` from `LSQ::recvTimingSnoopReq`, `lsq_impl.hh:419,433`).
  - The other uses (`:629`, `lsq_unit.hh:1063`) are InvisiSpec validation/expose paths, inactive under STT.
  - It touches no taint, delay, or STT logic.
- **Ours vs the authors':** our `se.py` glue forces `cpu.needsTSO = False` whenever `--scheme` is given (`configs/example/se.py:333`). The authors' `sample_scripts/run_stt.sh:19-20` uses `--needsTSO=1 --threat_model=Spectre --STT=1 --implicit_channel=1`, with `--caches --l2cache`. Second deviation found: our E3 runs have **no `--l2cache`**.
- **Expected effect on our tests:** none on taint or verdicts.
  - In single-core SE with classic caches nothing else sends invalidation snoops, so the TSO load squash never fires.
  - The one-store-in-flight rule only delays the drain of committed stores, so `lc`/`fnc` retire times and store-to-load forwarding (from SQ entries) are unchanged in most tests.
  - Small timing shifts are possible in tests with several committed stores.
  - x86 is TSO, so `needsTSO=1` is the architecturally faithful setting.
- **Recommendation:** match the authors, `needsTSO=1` and `--l2cache`.
  - This needs a config-glue change: set `needsTSO` from a new option instead of hard `False` at `se.py:333`, and add `--l2cache` to E3's `extra_args`.
  - Then re-check the E3 60-stem sample for identical verdicts.
  - Low risk, and it removes a deviation reviewers could question.

## Footprint model: the unified AMuLeT-comparison experiment (Anna, 2026-11-10)

**Purpose.** One fair, unbiased number for the paper: how many fewer tests SimSpect needs than AMuLeT to find each previously known bug that AMuLeT reports on the three defenses it shares with us (InvisiSpec UV1, CleanupSpec UV3/UV4/UV5, SpecLFB UV6), plus SimSpect's new jmp-shadow bug reported separately. The abstract uses the **minimum over the five known bugs**. STT is excluded (no shared bug claimed: AMuLeT's STT finding KV3 is out of our scope). Every choice below was made by Anna on 2026-11-09/10; the decisions are in `URGENT_TODO.md` (DECIDED 2026-11-09 and 2026-11-10 entries, TA-1..TA-8, FP-6, LP-1..LP-6).

### 1. Model (one model for all three defenses)

- `Footprint_6_u` = the footprint defense block of `STAGE1_alloy/models/{InvisiSpec,CleanupSpec,SpecLFB}_6.als` (identical in the three files: all three papers promise that a speculative load leaves no cache footprint), with FP-6's protection set, placed on the ultimate skeleton. Outside the defense block it is identical to `STT_6_u` and `Recon_6_u` (checked by hash, comments stripped).
- The three defense clauses: (1) speculation contract = after an unresolved branch (STT_6's; the `_stl` store-bypass variant is the second contract, for InvisiSpec-Futuristic, not in this run); (2) protection set = memory + the initial value of every register the test never writes (`Mem_s + (Reg_s - Instruction.outreg.opstate)`, FP-6; papers: CleanupSpec §2.1 p3 "from the memory or from a register", InvisiSpec p3-4, AMuLeT UV6 secret in a register p12), never released; (3) leakage = load addresses only (each paper's scope).
- Checked: the port is equivalent to the footprint block on the old skeleton under every perturbation at exactly 6 (0 counterexamples; a broken control finds one). Paper check of all clauses: `wiki/design/MODELS_AND_BUILDS.md` PART 8, "2026-11-10 — Paper check of the footprint models".

### 2. Generation (production path; skeletons from the first Alloy round)

- **Stage 1 (skeleton enumeration):** RI-minimal cores of the footprint model (`STAGE1_alloy/models/Footprint_6_u_core_ri.als`), every partition EXHAUSTED (k2 11, k3 1,400, all others incl. k6 0): 1,411 cores, 771 distinct exactly, 363 distinct at the pin level (363 Stage-1.5 runs).
- **Stage 1.5 (core-pinned loose stage):** per core, the loose-pin model (`_lpP`) at `for 9 but 8 Instruction, 16 Operand, 6 Int`: production minimality (every unresolved instruction and every state must matter; `_lpA` is off), at least one committed instruction, prefix-IX symmetry breaking (any length up to 8), the no-junk rule (every instruction connects to the transmitter), protection-set fold 8 deep (lint `check_bound_consistency.py` passes), and a path-style pin "contains Stage-1 core k" (every core edge may be realized by an rf/ddi path, so ALUs and memory hops may sit in between). No forcing predicates of any kind.
- **Enumeration order:** seeded random SAT4J order (`alloy_solver_order.run_random_xml`, `a6RandomModels`, walk p 0.05; one seed per job, seeds 1, 2, … per core), skeleton queue shuffled with seed 20261110, 25 instances / 300 s per job, adaptive per-core budget (the most novel core among those within min jobs + 2 gets the next job), cross-core and cross-seed fingerprint dedup. Stopped at 25,116 distinct programs after 1,327 jobs (310 cores with 4 jobs; 6 cores EXHAUSTED at 0, all others CAPPED; median 76 new programs per core). Provenance: corpus `PROVENANCE.json` (per-core jobs/instances/new, model hashes, code hashes, queue seed, CPU-s), `MANIFEST.tsv` (stem → core, seed), cafe1 `loosepin_regen/runs15/Footprint_6_u_lpPc/{cores.json,jobs.jsonl,SUMMARY.json,QUEUE_ORDER.json}`.
- **Stage 2 (fanout recipe v3, "The fanout pipeline v3 (all bugs), 2026-11-10"):** `runconfigs/regen_lp_20261110/stage2_fv3.jsonc` = fv2 vocabulary (default + inc, cmov_rr; `br_cmp`; `jmp_direct` p 0.5), value-neutral splices (p 0.75 on the transmitter path, 0.25 elsewhere) and 1-3 fillers, floating-point routing on all 16 xmm registers, **OTB transform off**; then `make_rmw_corpus.py --select eligible` (half of the eligible load-transmitter tests become value-neutral RMWs); then `make_jmp_corpus.py --placement uniform` p 0.5 (one direct jmp at a uniformly drawn position). All draws deterministic per (seed, stem) and recorded in each `.ann.json`.
- **Streaming:** each stage starts as soon as its input appears (cores → Stage 1.5 → Stage 2 → gem5). Tests are copied to the run node atomically (files first under temp names, then a manifest of verified stems); consumers only run manifest-listed stems whose provenance says recipe v3.
- **Corpus (READY 2026-11-10 10:48 UTC cafe1, 10:50 cafe3):** `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/testsets_twostage/Footprint_6_u_core_lpP_fv3/` on cafe1 and cafe3: 25,116 programs, 50,232 (stem, mode) tests (0 Stage-2 failures), RMW 20,588 tests (`rmw_stems.tsv`), jmp rows 25,352 (`jmp_stems.tsv`), 28 streamed batches. UV5 shape (not_taken, all stems): committed load with a produced address 24.9%, same-line pair 43.2%, full UV5 timing 2.75% (old flat 1.7% / 1.6%). Generation cost (TA-7): Stage 1 3,900 JVM-s, Stage 1.5 113,598 JVM-s, Stage 2 13,701 CPU-s, RMW 12,631, jmp 7,955: about 3.0 CPU-s per test. Production models installed as new files `STAGE1_alloy/models/Footprint_6_u_core_ri.als` + `Footprint_6_u_lpP.als`; one command `python3 twostage_lp.py --config runconfigs/twostage_lp/Footprint_6_u.jsonc --ws <dir>` (`make twostage-lp`). Details: `wiki/design/TWO_PHASE_MODELS.md` '2026-11-10 — Loose-pin regeneration (regen-lp)'.

### 3. Runs (AMuLeT's exact configs, averaged over settings)

- **Builds and configs:** the AMuLeT artifact builds at AMuLeT's own gem5 arguments (the parity configs; each `extra_args` line cites `executor.py` / the YAML): InvisiSpec-Futuristic (Ruby MESI_Two_Level, L1D 32 kB/8, TBEs 256, 2 GHz, `--access-backing-store`), CleanupSpec (Ruby, TBEs 256), SpecLFB (classic caches, L1D 32 kB/8, L1I 32 kB/2, L2 2 MB/8, DTB 64, BiModeBP, `--scheme=Speclfb`). Configs: `runconfigs/firstdet_final_20261110/` (copies of `runconfigs/regen_lp_20261110/T{1,2,3}ra_*`).
- **Per-test random settings (no best-of):** cache setup `random_all` = one of warm / cold / full per test (InvisiSpec warm / full, since clflush is a no-op there), sha256(seed, stem), seed `simspect-cache-random-v1`; timing = one whole-test point drawn uniformly from the 3x3 grid {2500, 5000, 10000} (every unresolved branch) x {0, 500, 2500} (every resolved branch), so the earlier fixed setting (5000, 0) is one of the nine points (`sweep.timing_grid`, runs T1g/T2g/T3g, Anna 2026-11-10); one gem5 run per test, recorded per record. The earlier interim runs T*rt drew stalls per branch from the same sets. Harness hooks (branch force, stalls) as in every SimSpect run; disclosed in the paper (TA-8).
- **Hygiene:** `TMPDIR=/tmp` (H-STK); all transmitter kinds; same seed-20261109 order for all three defenses.
- **Classification:** existing per-bug rules (`STAGE3_gem5/timing/classify_hits.py --testset`, `classify_cs_rmw.py`, the UV5 signature from bug-investigate-uv5). Exclusions that only lower SimSpect's counts: hits caused by the padding RMW past `.text` (H-PAD), setup-block events under `full`, UV3/E→M credited only to the test's own RMW. Unexplained hits are reported, never counted as known bugs.

### 4. AMuLeT baseline (measured, T5)

- AMuLeT's own fuzzer (repo c871bdf, Python 3.11.8 venv `/opt/amulet-venv`), its YAMLs and `benchmark_all.sh` arguments with only the gem5 paths changed, contract CT-SEQ, default seeds, 32 instances × 200 programs × 140 inputs per defense, on cafe1 (dedicated), against the same AE binaries (SimSpect's print-only hooks are inactive without debug flags).
- Unit: AMuLeT's test case = one program on one input (`src/benchmark.py:230`). Every violation replayed and classified with AMuLeT's own analysers (`classify_t5.py --all`, `cleanupspec_analysis.py`), giving per-bug counts.
- Pooled test cases per violation (programs × 140): UV1 3,318; UV3 3,794; UV4 224,000; UV5 224,000; UV6 5,978; jmp shadow 19,040 (47 of AMuLeT's 248 SpecLFB violations are jmp shadow, never attributed by AMuLeT). Detail: "T5: AMuLeT on cafe1" and "AMuLeT per-bug first detection".

### 5. Metric

- Per bug: SimSpect expected tests to first detection = (N+1)/(K+1) (K detections in N tests, random order); also the first-detection position in the seed-20261109 order. Multiple = AMuLeT pooled test cases per violation ÷ SimSpect expected tests. Same estimator on both sides (total tests ÷ detections), which counts AMuLeT's instances that never found a rare bug.
- Abstract number: the minimum multiple over UV1, UV3, UV4, UV5, UV6. Paper body: the per-bug table and figure (`ISCA-27/tikz/firstdet_bars*`), the geometric mean, and the CPU comparison including SimSpect's amortized generation cost (Alloy + Stage 2 CPU ÷ corpus size, TA-7).
- Interim (before this run, mixed corpora): minimum ≈ 47× (UV5 on the old flat corpus, 2 detections in 14,343 tests, 95% interval ≈ 4×-110×); UV1 ≈ 3,300×, UV3 ≈ 244×, UV4 ≈ 1,900×, UV6 ≈ 2,800×.

### 6. Known limits and follow-ups

- STL (store-to-load / store-bypass speculation) is not in this run: TODO, run the `_stl` footprint variants later (InvisiSpec-Futuristic, SpecLFB mUSL).
- RMW and jmp are post-passes in this run; making them first-class vocabulary needs the `parsexml.py` diff `STAGE2_compilation/proposed/v3_vocab_rmw_jmp.diff` (TODO VOCAB-RJ, Anna applies later after a byte-identical check and a gem5 smoke).
- A non-minimal corpus (no RR/RS minimality, only the core pin and connectivity) is a later TODO, so the generator is not barred from any shape. **Done 2026-11-10 (nonmin): part 9 below.**
- Multi-branch shapes are absent under production minimality (proven for STT_6_u at but 8), which matters for STT-side bugs, not this comparison.

### 7. Results

**Final at the 12:01 UTC deadline (firstdet-final, 2026-11-10).** Multiples = AMuLeT pooled test cases per violation ÷ SimSpect expected tests (N+1)/(K+1).

The static shape assumptions (D only, C only) were both falsified, hence the stratified estimator.

**Complete run (firstdet-final, classified 2026-11-10 14:50 UTC): every bug on the FINAL protocol.** Protocol: AMuLeT's exact parity configs; per test one cache setup from `random_all` (warm/cold/full; InvisiSpec warm/full) and one whole-test timing point uniform over unresolved {2500, 5000, 10000} × resolved {0, 500, 2500} (`sweep.timing_grid`, includes the old (5000, 0)); corpus `Footprint_6_u_core_lpP_fv3`. CleanupSpec (T2g) = all 50,232 tests (cafe3 25,142 + cafe1 25,090, 0 errors, 0 reruns), so UV3/UV4/UV5 are exact over the whole corpus; InvisiSpec (T1g) and SpecLFB (T3g) = the first 4,000 of the seed-20261109 order. Report `firstdet_final/report/final/complete.{md,json}`.

- **Minimum over the five known bugs (tests): UV5, 22.3x, exact (K = 4 in all 50,232; 95% Clopper-Pearson 4.9-46x).** Next: UV3 141x. On CPU, AMuLeT is cheaper for UV3 (0.42x) and UV5 (0.07x); SimSpect is cheaper for UV1, UV4, UV6, jmp shadow.
- **UV5 hits (all cold):** not_taken/inst-021207 (class D, timing (10000, 2500)), taken/inst-021207 (same point), not_taken/inst-007645 (class C, (2500, 0)), taken/inst-007645 (same point). Two programs, each detected in both branch modes. Static class C holds 2 of the 4 and class D 1, so the static shapes do not bound UV5 (the stratified deadline estimate, 23x, agrees with the exact 22.3x). UV5 never fired under warm or full.
- **CPU basis:** SimSpect total CPU per test (build + gem5 + checker) InvisiSpec 6.41, CleanupSpec 6.19, SpecLFB 4.72 (gem5 alone 1.31 / 2.27 / 1.34; cafe3/cafe1 under 60 workers, an upper bound), plus 3.02 CPU-s per test amortized generation (`PROVENANCE.json`: Alloy 117,498 CPU-s / 50,232 tests + Stage 2 and post-passes 34,287 CPU-s / 50,232 tests). AMuLeT = pooled CPU-s per violation of its own fuzzer (T5).
- **Other CleanupSpec classes (reported, not targets):** own-RMW E→M 472, unattributed 96, CS1 4; excluded H-PAD padding UV3 47 and E→M 7. SpecLFB other: walk_order 666, `replayed_after_safe(jmp_in_shadow)` 404. InvisiSpec warm tests: other_L1D/DTLB/L2/DIR footprints only.
- **Where:** cafe3 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/firstdet_final/` `T1g_*`, `T3g_*`, `T2g_*_merged` (merged from `T2g_*` + `T2g_*_cafe1`), `report/final/` (`complete.md/json`, `uv5_final_stats.txt`); cafe1 holds the `_cafe1` half.

**Final numbers at Anna's deadline (firstdet-final, classified 2026-11-10 12:01 UTC; AMuLeT's exact parity configs, `random_all` cache setup, per-test random timing; one corpus `Footprint_6_u_core_lpP_fv3`, 50,232 tests).**

| bug | defense | data (protocol, rows) | K/N | expected tests (N+1)/(K+1) | detecting setups | AMuLeT pooled test cases | multiple (tests) | multiple (CPU, incl. 3.0 s/test amortized generation) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| UV1 | InvisiSpec-Fu | T1g (grid), seed-20261109 prefix | 589/1,206 | 2.05 (first at #1) | full only | 3,318 | 1,620x | 13.7x |
| UV3 | CleanupSpec | T2rt (per-branch random), all classified, 2 hosts | 900/21,769 | 24.2 | cold 849, full 51 | 3,794 | 157x | 0.45x (AMuLeT cheaper) |
| UV4 | CleanupSpec | T2rt, all classified | 266/21,769 | 81.5 | cold 103, warm 66, full 97 | 224,000 | 2,750x | 7.9x |
| UV5 | CleanupSpec | T2g (grid), stratified over all 50,232 (below) | 3 hits in 23,126 run | 9,645 (95% 2,530-24,100) | cold only | 224,000 | **23x (95% 9.3-89x)** | — |
| UV6 | SpecLFB | T3g (grid), seed prefix | 345/1,721 | 4.98 (first at #1) | cold 170, full 169, warm 6 | 5,978 | 1,200x | 3.0x |
| jmp shadow (new) | SpecLFB | T3rt, all classified | 1,476/9,950 | 6.74 | cold 1,170, full 306 | 19,040 | 2,830x | 6.7x |

- **Minimum over the five known bugs: UV5, about 23x (95% 9.3-89x)**; next UV3 157x. On CPU, UV3 is the one bug where AMuLeT is cheaper (0.45x on a loaded node, generation included).
- **UV5 estimator (stratified, no shape assumption; `STAGE3_gem5/timing/uv5_stratified.py`, cafe3 `firstdet_final/report/uv5_latest.json`).** Stratum S1 = the static same-line (class C, includes D) not_taken stems of `uv5_timing_scan.py` (10,849; all run under T2g): K1 = 2. Stratum S2 = every other test (39,383), run in the seed-20261109 order (a uniform sample): K2 = 1 in 12,277. Expected UV5 in the corpus = K1 + K2 × 39,383/12,277 = 5.21, rate 1.04e-4, expected tests 9,645; 95% interval = exact Poisson on K2 scaled, plus K1 → 2.1-19.9 UV5, multiple 9.3-89x. Hits (all cold): not_taken/inst-021207 (D, timing (10000, 2500)); not_taken/inst-007645 (C, not D, (2500, 0)); **taken**/inst-007645 (S2, (2500, 0)). So neither the D-only nor the C-only shape assumption holds (the C-only estimate would be 2/50,232 = 13.4x; the D-only one 8.9x; both superseded). UV5 never fired under warm or full; uv5-footprint: CleanupSpec's L1D uses random replacement, so `full` rarely evicts the test's lines.
- **D-only diagnostic T2u5 (Anna):** the 690 D stems once each, 50% old fixed parity timing / 50% per-branch random: 1 UV5 (inst-021207, cold, fixed); inst-021207 also gives UV5 under per-branch random timing (its only branch is unresolved, so the two timings coincide). The old timing is not what restores UV5; it is just rare (about 1 in 230 cold D tests).
- **Protocols (do not mix):** `T*rt` = per-branch random timing (unresolved {2.5k, 5k, 10k} per branch, resolved {0, 500, 2.5k} per branch), 10:01-11:20 UTC, InvisiSpec 7,028, CleanupSpec 30k (21,769 classified), SpecLFB 9,950. **`T*g` = the final protocol**: one whole-test timing point uniform over unresolved {2500, 5000, 10000} × resolved {0, 500, 2500} (`sweep.timing_grid`, includes the old (5000, 0)), started 11:25 UTC; paused at 11:38 for the UV5 run (T1g 1,293 and T3g 1,834 tests of their 4,000 prefix; resume with the launcher, done tests are skipped); T2g ran D, then C, then the rest of the seed order on cafe3 + cafe1 until 11:54 (23,126 tests). UV3/UV4 in T2g (953 and 199 of 23,126) are not quoted because T2g's order is C-first, not a uniform sample.
- **Generation CPU (TA-7):** from the corpus `PROVENANCE.json`: Alloy (Stage 1 + 1.5 JVM seconds) 101k CPU-s / (2 × 20,505 programs) + Stage 2 and post-passes 23k CPU-s / 33,810 pairs = 3.0-3.15 CPU-s per test, added to SimSpect's 5-7 total CPU-s per test (cafe3 loaded with 60 workers, so SimSpect CPU is an upper bound).
- **Caveats:** UV1 is the share of InvisiSpec tests that drew `full` (TA-6: it measures the setup); the per-bug signatures differ between the SimSpect and AMuLeT classifiers (TA-8); UV1/UV6 prefixes are short (1.2k/1.7k) but their rates are high; UV3/UV4/jmp come from the per-branch-random protocol until T2g/T3g finish a uniform sample.
- **Where:** cafe3 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/firstdet_final/` (`launch.log`; run dirs `T{1,2,3}rt_*`, `T{1,2,3}g_*`, `T2g_*_merged`, `T2u5_*_merged`, `T2g_ext_*` (idle, ext corpus never arrived); `uv5diag/` (D/C stem lists, scan copy, priority file, estimator); `report/`), cafe1 the same tree for the `_cafe1` halves. Code: `STAGE3_gem5/timing/{firstdet_stream,firstdet_report,firstdet_merge,firstdet_final_launch.sh,firstdet_sync_cafe1.sh,uv5_estimate,uv5_stratified,ext_priority,make_firstdet_fig_v2}.py`, `pipeline.py` (`_per_branch_resolved`, `_timing_mode`, `_timing_grid_point`), `classify_hits.py --incremental`, configs `runconfigs/firstdet_final_20261110/`. Figure: `ISCA-27/tikz/firstdet_bars_v2_body.tex` updated with these values, preview `_standalone_firstdet_bars_v2-1.png` refreshed.

**Snapshot for replication (2026-11-10, snapshot agent):** `experiments/2026-11-10_footprint_vs_amulet/` holds the frozen code of every stage (run-host copies + Mac diffs), the exact models (production + per-core pinned), every run config and leakage spec, the environment (gem5 binary md5/sha256 and source-tree hashes, hooks ver=2, Alloy jar, Java, AMuLeT c871bdf + venv freeze, container image), the per-record results/classifications/reports of T*rt, T2u5 and the first T*g tests, the AMuLeT T5 summaries and per-bug JSONs, example traces (UV5 inst-021207 + 3 per bug), replication commands for each step (README), and `SHA256SUMS`. Corpus tarball (gitignored): `data/footprint_vs_amulet_20261110_corpus.tar.gz`, sha256 `08effe5d29a95bfd4f4b7130817c8d434914fe2b3c02de7c51b57685af4b3032`, copy on cafe1 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/snapshots/`. Taken while T*g was running; **refreshed**: results re-copied 14:52 UTC after T*g finished (`recopy_results.sh`), and at 18:58 UTC the README gained the same-estimator table (18×, DG-2) and `results/figure_final/` (the 18× figure files); `SHA256SUMS` regenerated.

### 8. Threats to validity and diligence (2026-11-10)

Diligence pass (agent diligence, for Anna; read-only on code, no git, no classifier or checker edits). Each check has a verdict and its evidence. Work dir: cafe3 (and cafe1) `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/bugwork/diligence/` (`uv5/` traces, raw records, reruns; `scripts/`); script copies in the repo at `STAGE3_gem5/timing/diligence_20261110/`. Checks 1-5 were run 13:55-14:30 UTC on the deadline numbers and on snapshots of the resumed T1g/T2g/T3g; check 6 ran 14:19-14:53. **Update at 14:55 for the complete run above (UV5 4/50,232, 22.3x):** the 4th hit (taken inst-021207) was verified and rerun the same way (below), the stratified estimator is now superseded by the exact count, and the T2rt-merge caveat no longer affects quoted numbers (UV3/UV4 now come from the complete T2g). Decisions raised: `URGENT_TODO.md` DG-1..DG-6.

**Answer first.** All four UV5 hits are real CleanupSpec over-cleaning, and each reproduces exactly. No unclassified hit hides a UV3, UV4 or UV5. Three things could change the headline. (1) **The unit.** 22-23x is in tests, where one test is one program on one input. A SimSpect test costs 3.5 CPU-s on a dedicated node (6.2-6.5 loaded); an AMuLeT test case costs 0.028 CPU-s. In CPU, AMuLeT finds UV5 about 10x more cheaply: the multiple is 0.10x including generation and 0.18x execution only, on dedicated nodes (DG-1). (2) **The UV5 interval.** The complete run's 4.9-46x (Clopper-Pearson) covers only SimSpect's uncertainty. Counting AMuLeT's own K = 4 too gives about 3.3-96x (exact conditional rate-ratio test), with a point estimate of 17.8x when both sides use the same estimator. The 4 hits are 2 programs, each found in both modes (DG-2). (3) **The over_clean window rule.** Every UV5 record is `inconsistent`, and option (ii) of the open "UV5 / over_clean window" DECIDE would remove all of them (DG-3).

**Check 1. UV5 hits, re-verified from the kept traces. Verdict: real UV5 (CleanupSpec too-much-cleaning), all four. The first three each reproduce 4/4 (2 reruns on each of cafe3 and cafe1), and the 4th 1/1 (cafe1), with the same draw (cold) and timing point.**

- Method: own trace parse (`bugwork/diligence/uv5/pvseq.py` + grep of the `line=0x75c0` hook events) of the kept traces `T2g_*/traces/<mode>/<stem>.pipeview.txt.gz`. The checker record was not trusted for this.
- not_taken inst-021207 (cafe3, cold, (10000, 2500); only branch seq 3139 `JNB` at 0x404429, forced not_taken, resolves 22570500). S = seq 3151, pc 0x40444d = the transmitter `ld rsi,[rsi+rdx]`, retire 0 (squashed). S misses L1D 17401000, misses L2 17405000, memory data 17476500, L1D fill 0x75c0 at 17483500 (E). U = seq 3124, pc 0x404403 `ldfp xmm4_low,[rsi+rdx]`, older than the branch, retires 17568000 (U is the lc). U issues at 17483500 and hits 0x75c0 at 17484000 (st=E), 500 ticks after S's fill. After the squash: S's `cleanup_req` at 22571500, then `L1D:cleanup_inv … ev=Inv_CLEANUP st=E` at 22572000, then `L2:cleanup_inv … Cleanup_Invalidate_clean` at 22586000. So the line U used is removed from L1D and L2 (the line was cold, so S's GETS installed it in L2 too).
- not_taken inst-007645 (cafe1, cold, (2500, 0)): S = seq 3191 pc 0x40446d `ld r9,[r12+r13]` (not the transmitter, which store-forwards), squashed; L1D fill 0x75c0 at 17496000. U = seq 3175 pc 0x404441 `ldfp xmm8_low,[rsi+r13]`, older than branch seq 3184, retires 17569500, hits at 17497000. `Inv_CLEANUP st=E` at 18674000, L2 cleanup_inv at 18688000.
- taken inst-007645 (cafe1, cold, (2500, 0)): the same mechanism. S = seq 3194 pc 0x404479, fill at 17496000. U = seq 3178 pc 0x404443, retires 17569000, hits at 17497000. Inv_CLEANUP at 18674000, L2 at 18688000. Its branch differs (mispredict_taken), so it is a different test of the same program.
- taken inst-021207 (cafe1, cold, (10000, 2500); 4th hit, complete run): S = seq 3151, pc 0x404457, retire 0. U = seq 3127, pc 0x404405 `ldfp xmm4_low,[rsi+rdx]`, retires 17552500. The classifier's chain: S fills 0x75c0 at 17476000, U hits at 17477000, cleanup_inv at 22556500 (L1D + L2). Retire ticks are from my own parse. Rerun `bugwork/diligence/uv5/rerun4_cafe1/`: hit, over_clean, cold.
- Rerun: `firstdet_stream.py --only-file` (the three pairs) with the T2g config and code (`firstdet_final/code`), `TMPDIR=/tmp`, 3 workers, twice on each host (`bugwork/diligence/uv5/rerun_{cafe3,cafe1}_{1,2}/`). 12/12 runs: hit, `over_clean`, cache_setup cold, the same timing_point and stall_points as the campaign row.
- Notes: (a) In all three the record is `inconsistent` (count rule says over_clean, net replay calls 0x75c0 outside W), because S's fill precedes lc_retire and U is the lc (see DG-3). (b) In all three, S and U read the same stack address 0x7fffffffe5c0. The address is a constant slot in zero-filled memory, so the test shows the over-cleaning mechanism but leaks no secret (as with inst-039102). (c) All three U are floating-point-routed loads (`MOVQ_XMM_M` / `ldfp`), so these detections pass through the FP routing vocabulary (TARGETING_AUDIT S3, disclose). (d) The 4 hits are 2 programs (inst-007645 and inst-021207), and each fires in both modes.

**Check 2. CleanupSpec hits classified as nothing. Verdict: no class hides a UV3/UV4/UV5. Leaving them out does not bias the comparison.**

- **`unattributed` items** (T2rt merged 54 items in 54 hits, T2g merged 59; all `under_clean` L1D, all under `full`). They never stand alone: every one sits in a hit that is also `UV3` (51 / 57 hits) or `UV3_padding` (3 / 2), so no K changes. What they are: the victim of the same squashed RMW's UV3 install. Under `full` the set is full, so S's store miss to the frame line (0xd5c0) evicts a sweep line (for example T2g nt inst-001446: `L1D:evict line=0xd5c0 seq=1634 pc=0x402abd victim=0x35c0`, where seq 1634 is the RMW transmitter). The cleanup is skipped (nohitinfo), so the victim is never restored. `classify_cs_rmw.py`'s UV3 rule requires every offending event to be a `fill req=st`, so the victim item (an evict event) falls through to `unattributed`.
- **CF-2 (the 14 unattributed hits under `full` in old T2f)** is the same thing. All 14 are `UV3+unattributed` (12), `EM_padding+UV3+unattributed` (1) or `UV3_padding+unattributed` (1), and in each the unattributed line's actor S equals the UV3 or padding actor (`results_fv2/T2f_STT_6_fv2fp_rmw__firstdet/classes_rmw.jsonl`). It is not a new CleanupSpec gap. Proposed classifier fix (not applied): attribute an `under_clean` victim item to the cause of the fill whose `cause=` id evicted it.
- **`over_clean` without a claim:** 0 in every CleanupSpec run with raw records (T2rt, T2g, T2u5 on both hosts; results_fv2 T2/T2c/T2f/T2r; oldfanout T2r). Every over_clean record has `claimed_by_older ≥ 1`. The "2 records without a claim" from the brief were not found in any of these.
- **`no_evidence`** (T2rt merged 9 hits: 7 cold, 1 warm, 1 full): these are hits that finished after the evidence pass. The report's snapshot cut drops them and every row after the first one (239 rows), from both K and N, so no K or N bias.
- **`EM_rmw`** (CS-EM, T2rt 179, T2g 208) and **`CS1`** (T2g 2, `full`): known causes, reported separately and never credited (see Check 3 for the effect).
- Why no class can hide UV5: UV5 is the only cause whose offending item is `over_clean` (something removed that should stay). Every unclassified item is `under_clean`. UV4 has its own skip event (`why=split`), which is never in an unattributed item's evidence.

**Check 3. Classification symmetry between the tools. Verdict: no asymmetry changes the minimum by more than 2x. Most go against SimSpect. One oracle difference is fundamental and must be disclosed.**

- UV5. SimSpect needs an `over_clean` item with a claim by an OLDER, committed load plus S's `cleanup_inv` (`classify_hits` → `cleanupspec_skip_evidence` `UV5_older_load`). AMuLeT (`src/cleanupspec_analysis.py`, read on cafe1, c871bdf) needs exactly 1 differing L1D line, plus an `LD` and a `CLEANUP` on that line in the other run's protocol trace. It checks neither age nor commit, and the priority is UV3 > UV4 > UV5. So AMuLeT's predicate is looser, which favors AMuLeT, and its priority masks UV5 when UV3 is present. Non-exclusive (`cs_nonexclusive.json`): 5 UV5 violations instead of 4, so pooled 1,280 programs instead of 1,600, and the multiple is **18.6x instead of 23.2x (factor 1.25)**. Masking beyond that is bounded: 236 of 6,400 AMuLeT programs (3.7%) carry UV3, so unless UV5 is strongly correlated with UV3 the hidden UV5 rate is a few percent.
- UV3. SimSpect credits only the test's own RMW transmitter. It excludes CS-EM (`EM_rmw`, 179 in T2rt), H-PAD and other actors. AMuLeT's `is_rwm_bug` (every differing line has a `ST` in one run only) would plausibly also count the CS-EM pattern (a squashed RMW store upgrading S's line), but this was not tested on traces. Counting EM_rmw as UV3 would raise SimSpect's UV3 multiple about 1.2x (157x to about 188x). Against SimSpect.
- UV4. Non-exclusive AMuLeT gives 6 violations instead of 4, so 2,750x becomes 1,830x (factor 1.5). UV1 and UV6 are far from the minimum.
- **Oracle (disclose; DG-6).** SimSpect flags a per-run footprint violation (the defense's promise), even when the line does not depend on any secret (all three UV5 tests use one constant stack slot). AMuLeT flags a relational violation: two contract-equivalent inputs must end in different cache states, which implies input dependence. So a SimSpect detection is "the mechanism fired", and an AMuLeT detection is "the mechanism fired AND was observable across inputs". The paper should say so. It is not a factor that can be measured from the existing runs.

**Check 4. UV1 under `full`. Verdict: correct. The SETUP strip removes only sweep events, and every counted UV1 hit has a squashed test-load eviction.**

- `classify_hits.strip_setup` removes an offending event only when its actor pc (event pc, else the seq's pipeview pc) is in `[cache_setup_info.begin, begin + delta)`. An event with no resolvable pc is kept, never stripped.
- T1g snapshot (2,162 rows, 1,585 classified): UV1 781, all `full`, and all 781 are also `UV1_transient`, so UV1 = UV1_transient. Deep check of the first 150 UV1 hits (own trace parse, `uv1_full_check.py`): of the in-record specld evictions, 4,038 are by setup-block actors (committed sweep loads), all of which the strip removes, 250 by squashed test loads and 28 by committed test loads. 150/150 hits have at least one squashed test-load eviction. Warm tests (1,100) carry no UV1 (their "hits" are the known DIR/DTLB footprint events).
- Caveat already in CF-1/TA-6: under `full`, UV1 fires on essentially every test that draws `full`, so UV1's multiple measures the setup, not the generator.

**Check 5. Units and estimators. Verdict: the arithmetic is correct. Caveats: the test unit (DG-1), the UV5 interval (DG-2), and the stale T2rt merge (DG-4, now moot for quoted numbers).**

- AMuLeT side, from the run logs (cafe1 `timing/amulet_t5/runs/*/benchmark-out-*/info.txt`, `perbug/out/violations_*.json`): 6,400 programs per defense. The throughput check holds: CleanupSpec 1,301.64 test cases/s × 688 s ≈ 896,000 = 6,400 × 140, so 140 test cases per program. Violations (all replayed): CleanupSpec 246 (UV3 236, UV4 4, UV5 4, other 2), InvisiSpec 271 (UV1 270), SpecLFB 248 (UV6 150, jmp shadow 47 by perbug). Pooled programs: 23.7 / 27.1 / 1,600 / 1,600 / 42.7 / 136, which matches `firstdet_report.AMULET`.
- SimSpect N: every row in the scored dirs is `status: ok` (T1g, T2g, T3g, T*rt on both hosts; 0 files in `errors/`). Error rows would count as tests (`firstdet_report.py` docstring), which would only lower SimSpect's rates. Tests whose window did not open still count as tests, which is also conservative.
- **T2rt merge is stale (DG-4).** `T2rt_*_merged` = 22,008 rows (cafe3 15,896 + cafe1 6,112) from a merge taken about 11:00. The hosts ran 33,156 T2rt tests in all (cafe3 19,572, cafe1 13,584), so 11,148 were never merged or classified, including 2 UV5 hits. UV3 and UV4 are quoted from the merged 21,769. These rows are a stream-order snapshot (core-batch order), not a seed-order prefix: unbiased in expectation, clustered by core. Not the minimum, so the headline is unaffected.
- **UV5 stratified arithmetic** (`uv5_stratified.py`, `report/uv5_latest.json`): E[K] = 2 × 10,849/10,849 + 1 × 39,383/12,277 = 5.21; rate 1.04e-4; 9,645 tests; 224,000/9,645 = 23.2x. All correct. The estimator is N/K-style (MLE), not (N+1)/(K+1) like the other rows; the difference is small here. Intervals (`uv5_intervals.py`): published 9.3-89x (K1 fixed, exact Poisson on K2 only); both strata Poisson (Fay-Feuer weighted-sum gamma) 3.3-89x; Jeffreys Monte Carlo 7.8-80x; including AMuLeT's K = 4 (95% 87,500-822,000 test cases per UV5) 5.9-125x (median 27.7x).
- **Independent cross-check:** T2rt before the 11:20 priority restart (stream order, untargeted, both hosts) ran 32,267 tests with 2 UV5 (inst-007645 in both modes, cold, stall 10000). That gives (N+1)/(K+1) = 10,756 tests, 20.8x (MLE 13.9x, 95% 1.7-50x). This is consistent with 23x. inst-021207 also fired in T2rt's post-restart (priority) segment.
- Strata choice: S1 (static class C) was fixed before T2g ran, but the stratified estimator was chosen after the 3 hits were seen (the D-only and C-only assumptions were falsified by them). The stratified estimator is unbiased for any fixed strata, so this is a disclosure point, not an error. Superseded at 14:50: the complete T2g gives K = 4/50,232 exactly (22.3x), in line with the stratified 23x and the T2rt cross-check. For the exact count, SimSpect-only Clopper-Pearson is 4.9-46x. With AMuLeT's K = 4 (Poisson) included it is 3.3-96x (conditional binomial on 4 vs 4) or 4.4-71x (Jeffreys Monte Carlo), point 17.8x when both sides use rate = K/N. The table's 22.3x comes from mixing (N+1)/(K+1) for SimSpect with N/K for AMuLeT, a small asymmetry of 1.25x against AMuLeT at K = 4. Effective independence is 2 programs, not 4 tests.

**Check 6. Clean CPU numbers (dedicated cafe1, 14:19-14:53 UTC). Verdict: SimSpect's per-test CPU on a dedicated node is about 1.8x below the loaded numbers. AMuLeT still finds UV5 (0.10x) and UV3 (0.6-0.7x) more cheaply. For every other bug SimSpect is cheaper on CPU as well (4-20x).**

- Run: the first 1,000 tests of the seed-20261109 order per defense (`firstdet_stream.py --seed-prefix 1000`), T1g/T2g/T3g configs and code (`firstdet_final/code`), random_all + grid timing, `TMPDIR=/tmp`, 3 rows × 2 workers = 6 on an otherwise idle cafe1 (load about 2-6). Output: cafe1 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/bugwork/diligence/cpu/T{1,2,3}g_cpu1000/` (`run.log`, `results.jsonl`, `driver.log`); summary `scripts/cpu_summary.py` (repo copy `STAGE3_gem5/timing/diligence_20261110/cpu_summary.py`). 3,000/3,000 `ok`.
- Dedicated CPU-s per test, mean, as total (build + gem5 + checker) / gem5 only: InvisiSpec 3.60 / 0.68 (full 5.18, warm 2.06); CleanupSpec 3.49 / 1.14 (cold 3.84, full 4.06, warm 2.66); SpecLFB 2.62 / 0.66. The complete-run table used 6.41 / 6.19 / 4.72 from the loaded hosts.
- CPU multiples = AMuLeT pooled CPU-s per violation ÷ (SimSpect expected tests × dedicated CPU-s per test), first with the 3.0 CPU-s/test amortized generation (TA-7), then execution only in brackets. Complete-run expected tests: UV1 2.00 → 20.4x (37.5x); **UV3 27.0 → 0.60x (1.11x)**; UV4 83.7 → 11.3x (21.1x); **UV5 10,047 → 0.094x (0.18x)**; UV6 4.64 → 4.4x (9.4x); jmp 7.20 → 9.0x (19.4x). The deadline-table values give the same picture (UV5 0.098x, UV3 0.67x). Generation (3.0 s/test) is now the larger part of SimSpect's cost. AMuLeT's CPU-s were measured on cafe1 with 32 instances, so near-dedicated too.
- Minimum over the five known bugs on CPU: UV5, about 0.1x (AMuLeT about 10x cheaper). In tests it is UV5 at 22x. See DG-1.

### Dedicated CPU timing (TIME-1, 2026-11-15)

Agent timing, decision TIME-1 (Anna, 2026-11-15: re-time both tools under the same load, in parallel with the big-corpus rerun). One tool at a time on cafe2 (Xeon Gold 6226R, 2 × 16 cores, 64 hardware threads, turbo on), every process pinned to CPUs 0-31 (`taskset`; one hardware thread per physical core, so our own processes never share a core), about 16 busy threads for SimSpect and about 15 for AMuLeT. SimSpect numbers come only from the big corpus (Footprint A, unified pipeline). Work dir cafe2 `gem5_dev:/tests/timing_20261115/`; scripts `STAGE3_gem5/timing/time1_20261115/`; summary `time1_summary.{md,json}` there.

**Answer.** Per-test CPU on a dedicated node: SimSpect 3.69 / 3.74 / 2.78 CPU-s per test (InvisiSpec / CleanupSpec / SpecLFB), AMuLeT 0.076 / 0.022 / 0.015 CPU-s per test case (10.7 / 3.0 / 2.1 per program). Under matched load AMuLeT is 7% (InvisiSpec) to 22-23% (CleanupSpec, SpecLFB) cheaper per program than in T5 (32 instances on cafe1), which confirms that the T5 load inflated AMuLeT's CPU-s. Generation adds about 0.9 CPU-s per test (Alloy re-timed on the idle node + Stage 2), not the 2.1 that the overloaded-cafe3 PROVENANCE implies. With the big-corpus K/N, SimSpect is cheaper on CPU per detection for A-UV1 (27x with generation), A-UV4 (2.7x), A-UV6 (5.0x) and jmp shadow (9.8x); AMuLeT is cheaper for A-UV3 (0.44x) and A-UV5 (SimSpect found none in 50,232 tests: at most 0.017x).

**SimSpect (big corpus `Footprint_6_u_core_lpP_s2A`, 2026-11-15 22:27-22:40 UTC).** A fixed random sample of 1,000 (mode, stem) pairs (`make_sample.py`, `random.Random(20261115).sample` of the 26,550 manifest-listed, provenance-passing pairs complete on cafe3 at 22:25 UTC, while the corpus was still streaming; 985 distinct stems; 506 not_taken / 494 taken; all `ld` transmitters), the same 1,000 for all three defenses. Configs `configs/T{1,2,3}g_time1.jsonc` = `runconfigs/bigcorpus_20261115/T{1,2,3}g_Footprint_6_u_core_lpP_s2A.jsonc` with only paths changed; code = the bigcorpus snapshot (md5 of `pipeline.py`, `firstdet_stream.py`, `timing_driver.py`, `check_footprint.py`, `gem5_common.py`, `cache_setup.py` and the three configs identical on cafe2 and cafe3); gem5 binaries' md5 equal to `experiments/2026-11-10_footprint_vs_amulet/environment.md`; `firstdet_stream.py --only-file sample.tsv --parallel 16`, `TMPDIR=/tmp`. Per-test CPU = `os.wait4` rusage of the whole `pipeline.py gem5` tree (build + gem5 + checker), gem5-only from `TIMING_GEM5_LOG`, as in diligence check 6. 3,000/3,000 `ok`.

**Generation CPU (big corpus, re-timed).** The corpus `PROVENANCE.json` (complete, 23:56 UTC: 62,052 programs, 124,104 tests) records Stage 1.5 as the sum of per-job WALL seconds on cafe3 at load ~160-220 (210,855 s) and Stage 2 as RUSAGE CPU (46,727 CPU-s). Re-time: 16 Stage 1.5 jobs drawn at random (seed 20261115) from cafe3's `jobs.jsonl`, re-run on cafe2 with the same model files, seeds, cap 100, timeout 600 s, walk p 0.05, Xmx 4g, 16 at once (`gen_retime.py`, 23:51-23:54 UTC). Each produced the same instance count as on cafe3 (100 each; one cafe3 job had timed out at 86). Their solver wall time on the idle node summed to 1,568 s against 4,800 s on cafe3 (ratio 0.327; per job 0.24-0.75). Scaling the Alloy total by that ratio gives 68,900 s, i.e. 0.56 s per test, plus Stage 2 0.38 CPU-s per test: **about 0.93 CPU-s per test of generation** (uncorrected PROVENANCE: 2.08). The Stage 1 cores (1,411 RI-minimal cores of the same model) were reused, a one-off cost not counted here.

**CPU per detection (big-corpus K/N, agent bigcorpus's final report `cafe3:/tests/bigcorpus_20261115/firstdet/report/nov10_s2A/report.{md,json}`).** Same estimator on both sides: expected tests to a detection = (N+1)/(K+1). SimSpect CPU per detection = expected tests × CPU per test above (+ 0.93 CPU-s per test of generation in the last column); AMuLeT CPU per detection = (896,000+1)/(K_A+1) × CPU per test case above, K_A from T5 (all violations replayed). Multiple = AMuLeT ÷ SimSpect (> 1: SimSpect cheaper). Computed by `summarize_time1.py . --kn-json kn_bigcorpus.json --gen 0.93` (cafe2 `/tests/timing_20261115/time1_summary.{md,json}`).

| bug | defense | SimSpect K/N | SimSpect expected tests | AMuLeT K / expected test cases | multiple (tests) | SimSpect CPU-s per detection, execution | AMuLeT CPU-s per detection | multiple, execution only | multiple, with generation |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A-UV1 | InvisiSpec-Fu | 2,002/4,000 | 2.00 | 270 / 3,306 | 1,655x | 7.4 | 252 | 34x | 27x |
| A-UV3 | CleanupSpec | 1,274/50,232 | 39.4 | 236 / 3,781 | 96x | 147 | 81.5 | 0.55x (AMuLeT cheaper) | 0.44x (AMuLeT cheaper) |
| A-UV4 | CleanupSpec | 162/50,232 | 308 | 4 / 179,200 | 581x | 1,152 | 3,864 | 3.4x | 2.7x |
| A-UV5 | CleanupSpec | **0/50,232** | > 50,232 (bound) | 4 / 179,200 | **≤ 3.6x (bound)** | > 187,700 | 3,864 | **≤ 0.021x (bound)** | **≤ 0.017x (bound)** |
| A-UV6 | SpecLFB | 851/4,000 | 4.70 | 150 / 5,934 | 1,264x | 13.1 | 87.4 | 6.7x | 5.0x |
| jmp shadow (new) | SpecLFB | 527/4,000 | 7.58 | 47 / 18,667 | 2,463x | 21.1 | 275 | 13x | 9.8x |

- **A-UV5 is a bound, not an estimate.** SimSpect found no A-UV5 in all 50,232 big-corpus tests, so (N+1)/(K+1) = 50,233 is only a lower bound on SimSpect's expected tests, and every A-UV5 multiple is an upper bound: at most 3.6x in tests and at most 0.021x / 0.017x in CPU (AMuLeT at least ~50-60x cheaper on CPU for this bug).
- On CPU, SimSpect is cheaper for A-UV1, A-UV4, A-UV6 and jmp shadow, and AMuLeT is cheaper for A-UV3 (about 2x) and A-UV5 (at least ~50x). Walk order (657/4,000 on SpecLFB) has no AMuLeT count, so it has no multiple.
- The AMuLeT CPU per test case carries the two deviations below, both of which push it up (decision TIME-3); if a matched rerun lowers AMuLeT's CleanupSpec/SpecLFB CPU, these multiples drop by the same factor.

**Load records.** Before each phase `snap.sh` wrote `uptime`, the `top -bn1` header and the non-zombie gem5/java/python processes into the run's `run.log`. Before the first phase the node was idle (load 0.8, 99.6% idle; only old zombie processes and idle wait-loops from 2026-11-09). A host monitor (`loadmon_host.sh`, every 30 s; log `logs/time1_loadmon.log`) recorded the whole session, summarized by `loadstats.py` (busy threads = (us + sy) / 100 × 64; first and last 60 s of each phase dropped):

| phase | window (UTC) | 1-min load mean (max) | busy hardware threads, mean (min-max) | other users |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SimSpect T1g | 22:27-22:31 | 14.6 (16.1) | 16.8 (16.2-17.3) | none |
| SimSpect T2g | 22:32-22:36 | 14.8 (15.8) | 16.4 (16.3-16.4) | none |
| SimSpect T3g | 22:37-22:40 | 14.5 (15.5) | 16.5 except one sample at 29.4 | dmendo, 2 processes for ~1 min |
| AMuLeT InvisiSpec | 22:47-23:28 | 19.1 (27.1) | 15.2 until 23:06, then 18-28 | dmendo, 5-6+ processes at ~100% from 23:06 |
| AMuLeT CleanupSpec | 23:29-23:42 | 26.8 (36.5) | 25.2 (15.3-33.9) | dmendo, 2-6+ processes |
| AMuLeT SpecLFB | 23:43-23:50 | 29.1 (32.4) | 26.7 (15.4-35.1) | dmendo, 0-6 processes |

**Deviations and fairness (read before quoting).**
- **Another user loaded the node during the AMuLeT runs.** From 23:06 a Slurm user (dmendo) ran 5-6 or more single-threaded Python processes at ~100% (the monitor lists only the top 6, so possibly more). They were not pinned, so some may have shared physical cores with AMuLeT (on CPUs 32-63). Effect measured from the instances' own CPU/progress samples: InvisiSpec 10.98 CPU-s per program before 23:06 vs 11.32 after (+3%). For CleanupSpec and SpecLFB the "after" segments are short run tails and not comparable. So the AMuLeT numbers are at most a few percent high (slightly in SimSpect's favour), and still 7-23% below T5. The SimSpect phases were essentially clean.
- **Busy threads were not equal.** InvisiSpec kept ~15 busy threads (14 instances); the CleanupSpec and SpecLFB instances keep their 4 gem5 IPC workers busier, so with the same 14 instances their own load reached ~25-32 threads in the first minutes (ours, pinned to 32 physical cores, so no hyperthread sharing among our own processes, but more active cores mean a lower turbo clock). The direction again inflates AMuLeT's CPU-s, not SimSpect's. A fully matched rerun would use ~7-8 instances for CleanupSpec and SpecLFB.
- **SimSpect sample from the corpus as streamed by 22:25 UTC** (26,550 of the final 124,104 tests, core-batch order), not from the finished corpus.
- **Generation:** the Alloy correction is solver wall time on an idle pinned core, which approximates CPU (single-threaded JVM; GC threads not counted), from 16 jobs. The first gen_retime pass lost its rusage rows to a Python 3.8 incompatibility in the script (`os.waitstatus_to_exitcode`, fixed in the repo copy); the jobs themselves completed and their `SOLVER_ORDER.json` timings are what is used. It was not rerun, because cafe2 was handed over as gem5 capacity.

#### Fast path re-time (2026-11-16; Anna: "just do fast path timing from now on and use that", TIME-4)

**SimSpect CPU per test is now quoted with the fast path.** Setup: the TIME-1 sample (the same 1,000 (mode, stem) pairs per defense from `Footprint_6_u_core_lpP_s2A`), the time1 T1g/T2g/T3g configs with only `"fast_path": true` added, 16 workers pinned to CPUs 0-31; production rerun and fast path back to back on the same tests (`STAGE3_gem5/timing/time1_20261115/retime_fast.sh`, chain `fast_chain.sh`). Equivalence check (`fastpath_equiv.py driver`, 300 tests per defense) ran on cafe2: 300/300 identical, 0 harness errors, for each defense (`cafe2:/tests/timing_20261115/eq_fast/eq_T{1,2,3}g/`). The measurement moved to cafe3 (same CPU, Xeon Gold 6226R, 64 logical CPUs) because another Slurm user loaded cafe2; idle gate passed at 05:48:53 UTC (whole node 0.4% busy), run ended 06:05:23 UTC, no NOT_IDLE (`cafe3:/tests/timing_20261115/logs/fast_chain_cafe3.log`, summary `retime_fast/summary.{md,json}`, host load log `~/time1_loadmon_cafe3.log`).

| SpecLFB (T3g) | 2.725 | 1.296 | 2.10x | 1000/1000 | 470/470 |

The production rerun matches the 2026-11-15 TIME-1 numbers (3.69 / 3.74 / 2.78) within 2%. Fast-path CPU includes its workers' one-time startup spread over the tests.

**CPU per detection with the fast path** (same estimator on both sides: (N+1)/(K+1); AMuLeT N = 896,000 test cases, K from T5, CPU per test case 0.0761 / 0.0216 / 0.0147 from TIME-1 on cafe2; generation 0.93 CPU-s per test; computed by the main session). Multiple > 1 means SimSpect is cheaper. CleanupSpec rows use the final pooled unified-pipeline counts (main 50,232 + extra-new 90,932, and for A-UV5 also the three re-concretization rounds, 291,860 tests); InvisiSpec and SpecLFB use the main run.

| bug | SimSpect K/N | tests multiple | SimSpect CPU-s per detection (exec) | AMuLeT CPU-s per detection | CPU multiple, exec only | CPU multiple, with generation |

| A-UV1 | 2,002/4,000 | 1,655x | 2.7 | 252 | 94x | 55x |

Caveats: the AMuLeT per-test-case CPU is still the cafe2 TIME-1 measurement with the two deviations above (other-user load, unmatched busy threads on CleanupSpec/SpecLFB; TIME-3 open), which make AMuLeT look a few percent more expensive; the A-UV5 count rests on K = 5 from 3 programs (95% interval of the tests multiple 1.0-7.2x on SimSpect's side only).
