**Why:** the cafe2 TIME-1 AMuLeT runs had another Slurm user's load and unmatched busy threads on CleanupSpec/SpecLFB. **Setup:** cafe3 (same node and CPU as SimSpect's fast-path re-time, Xeon Gold 6226R, 64 logical CPUs), exactly T5 (c871bdf copy, `benchmark_t5.py` md5 fefd24d9…, `/opt/amulet-venv` with T5's pip freeze, AE gem5 md5s matched, its YAMLs/benchmark_all.sh args, CT-SEQ, 140 inputs per program, 200 programs per instance), pinned to CPUs 0-31; per-defense instance count auto-calibrated to ~16 busy threads (chain `scripts/amulet_chain.sh`, log `cafe3:/tests/timing_20261115/logs/amulet_chain_cafe3.log`): InvisiSpec R=14 (15.1 busy), CleanupSpec R=15 (16.5), SpecLFB R=13 (17.2); off-target calibration attempts discarded to `amulet/discard_calib/`. ## Per-test cost and setup amortization (2026-11-16)

Agent testcost. Anna (2026-11-16): "why does it take so long to do a simspect test ... how we can preserve setup easier like amulet does. see if possible." Scripts `STAGE3_gem5/timing/testcost_20261116/` (`profile_test.py`, `micro_gem5.py`, `fast_worker.py`, `summarize.py`, `run_profile.sh`, `run_fast.sh`; each has a docstring). Node data: cafe2 `gem5_dev:/tests/testcost_20261116/` (`prof_T*/`, `prod_T*/`, `fast_T*/`, `micro_T*/`, `logs/`, `summary.json`). A copy of the summary is at `STAGE3_gem5/timing/testcost_20261116/summary_20261116.json`. No production file was edited, and nothing is left running.

**Answer.** Simulating the test itself is only 2-3% of a SimSpect test's CPU (0.06-0.08 CPU-s). That is about what AMuLeT spends on a whole test case (0.015-0.076 CPU-s). Everything else is fixed overhead that every SimSpect test pays, while AMuLeT pays it once per program (140 test cases). In order of cost:
- **Trace parsing in Python, 34-49%.** Eight full passes over a 6-9 MB trace, and 70-83% of the trace's lines come from the Commit debug flag.
- **Reading the gem5 binary, 15-17%.** Each test reads the whole 0.6-1.1 GB binary to look for one string.
- **gem5 startup, 10-30%.** This is the config script plus Ruby/SimObject instantiation: 10% for InvisiSpec, 17% for SpecLFB, 30% for CleanupSpec.
- **gem5 writing the trace, 7-9%.**
- **Two Python interpreters plus imports per test, about 2-10%.**
- **as/ld/objdump/nm, 3-5%.**

I prototyped a fast path that keeps every verdict: a persistent worker, cached hook detection and one routed trace read. On 60 tests it gave result records identical to the production functions (60/60). It cuts the in-process CPU to 0.38 / 0.53 / 0.52 of production. Estimated per-test CPU on an idle node: **1.25 / 1.88 / 1.41 instead of 3.69 / 3.74 / 2.78** (InvisiSpec / CleanupSpec / SpecLFB), i.e. 3.0x / 2.0x / 2.0x faster. What remains is mostly gem5 itself: 0.74 / 1.49 / 0.82 CPU-s per test, dominated by startup. Going below that needs AMuLeT's design (many tests per gem5 process). That needs a gem5 C++ patch and changes the microarchitectural state each test starts from, so it is a decision for Anna (`URGENT_TODO.md` TC-2), not something done here.

**Method.**
- **Inputs.** TIME-1's code snapshot, configs and big-corpus testset on cafe2, unchanged (`/tests/timing_20261115/{code,configs,testsets}`; `pipeline.py`, `gem5_common.py` and `firstdet_stream.py` have the same md5 as the Mac tree). The tests are the first 20 (mode, stem) pairs of TIME-1's 1,000-pair sample, so TIME-1's idle-node totals are known for exactly these tests. They ran on T1g / T2g / T3g. Cache setups drawn: InvisiSpec 11 warm / 9 full; CleanupSpec and SpecLFB 7 warm / 5 cold / 8 full.
- **(1) In-process profile, `profile_test.py`.** It replays the per-test path of `pipeline.py gem5` in one process: `pipeline._enumerate_grid_for_corpus` + `_materialise_grid_run`, then `gem5_common.process_one` with `check_footprint.check_footprint`. Every function of `gem5_common` / `cache_setup` / `check_footprint` on that path is wrapped in an rusage timer that records exclusive own CPU plus child-process CPU. The test's captured gem5 command is then re-run four ways: unchanged; without debug flags; O3PipeView only; and an exit-only binary with the same flags.
- **(2) Production harness.** `firstdet_stream.py` → `timing_driver.run_one` (parallel 1) on the same 20 tests, for the total CPU including both Python interpreters.
- **(3) gem5 fixed costs, `micro_gem5.py`.** Median of 5 runs.
- **Load and scaling.** Another campaign started 48 workers on cafe2 at 02:21 UTC, so load was 50-75 on 64 hardware threads. All of my processes ran single-threaded at nice 19. Raw CPU-s came out 1.79x / 1.83x / 1.53x TIME-1's idle numbers for the same tests (`load_factor` in summary.json). The table therefore gives each build's measured shares times TIME-1's per-test mean (3.69 / 3.74 / 2.78).
- **Verdicts.** The profile reproduced TIME-1's verdict on 60/60 tests.

| where the CPU goes | what it is | InvisiSpec (T1g) | CleanupSpec (T2g) | SpecLFB (T3g) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| trace parsing, 8 passes | `parse_pipeview`, `parse_commit_ticks`, `parse_lsq`, `parse_commit_squashes`, `parse_branch_events` (= `parse_simspect_events`), `parse_pipeview_by_seq` in `process_one` (`gem5_common.py:1213-1253`), then `parse_simspect_events` + `parse_pipeview_by_seq` again in `check_footprint.evaluate_trace` (`check_footprint.py:492,502`) | 1.81 (49%) | 1.26 (34%) | 1.32 (48%) |
| gem5 binary scan | `binary_has_resolve_hooks` reads the whole gem5 binary (`f.read()`, `gem5_common.py:184-191`; 1.12 / 1.12 / 0.61 GB) once per checker process, i.e. once per test | 0.62 (17%) | 0.65 (17%) | 0.41 (15%) |
| gem5 startup | exit-only binary with the run's exact se.py flags, no trace (config, Ruby/SimObject instantiation, teardown) | 0.36 (10%) | 1.14 (30%) | 0.48 (17%) |
| gem5 trace writing | actual run minus the no-debug-flag run | 0.33 (9%) | 0.28 (7%) | 0.26 (9%) |
| gem5 simulating the test | no-debug-flag run minus startup (includes the cache-setup code) | 0.06 (1.5%) | 0.08 (2%) | 0.08 (3%) |
| harness | production total minus the in-process total: `timing_driver.py:109` starts `python3 pipeline.py`, which starts `python3 check_footprint.py` (`pipeline.py:811`); interpreters, imports, staging | 0.37 (10%) | 0.19 (5%) | 0.05 (2%, noisy; see below) |
| toolchain | `as`/`ld` (`gem5_common.py:145-147`, twice for cold/full), `objdump` x2-4, `nm` x1-3, `cache_setup.remap_annotation` | 0.10 (3%) | 0.11 (3%) | 0.13 (5%) |
| checker logic | `evaluate`, `execution_validity`, `branch_force_plan`, rest of `process_one` | 0.06 (2%) | 0.03 (1%) | 0.05 (2%) |
| **total (TIME-1 idle, per test)** | | **3.69** | **3.74** | **2.78** |

Numbers come from `summarize.py /tests/testcost_20261116 --time1 /tests/timing_20261115/simspect` (cafe2): the shares are the categories in `summary.json` `<R>.summary`, multiplied by the TIME-1 means.

- **The harness row is a residual, so it is noisy.** The profile and the production pass ran a few minutes apart under changing load; on SpecLFB the in-process sum came out larger than the production total for the full tests. A direct measurement under the same load (`prof_T1g/startup.json`): bare interpreter 0.03 CPU-s, `import pipeline` 0.08, `import check_footprint` 0.12. That is about 0.27 for both interpreters, roughly 0.15 on an idle node.
- **What the trace contains** (means over the 20 tests, `profile.jsonl` `trace`):
  - InvisiSpec: 9.1 MB, 105k lines, 83% Commit, 9.3k O3PipeView, 8.9k SimSpect hook lines.
  - CleanupSpec: 6.5 MB, 80k lines, 77% Commit.
  - SpecLFB: 6.3 MB, 76k lines, 70% Commit.
  - Full-cache-setup traces are 2.5-5x warm ones (InvisiSpec 188k vs 37k lines) because the 512-load sweep is traced too. That is why full tests cost 5.34 vs 2.13 CPU-s in TIME-1 (InvisiSpec).
- **Per-pass cost** (InvisiSpec, loaded node, per test):
  - `parse_simspect_events` (two calls): 1.16.
  - `parse_lsq`: 0.65. It finds nothing, because these configs trace only O3PipeView (plus Commit) and no LSQUnit lines; it is wasted work, and `check_footprint` ignores `lsq_by_pc` anyway.
  - `parse_pipeview_by_seq` (two calls): 0.52.
  - The two Commit parsers: 0.59.
  - `parse_pipeview`: 0.25.
- **The Commit flag is not free, and it is needed.** `run_gem5` always adds it (`gem5_common.py:394`, for `check_br`). `check_footprint` uses it only in the `fill_retire_from_commit` fallback, which supplies lc/fnc retire ticks missing from O3PipeView. That fallback fired in 140/1,000 InvisiSpec, 6/56 CleanupSpec and 0/470 SpecLFB TIME-1 hit records (`retire_from_commit_trace` in `raw/*/*.json`). Dropping the flag would save about 0.06-0.1 CPU-s of gem5 trace writing and most of the trace lines, but it would change the window for those tests (DECIDE TC-3).
- **gem5 fixed cost** (`micro_gem5.py`, loaded node, InvisiSpec / CleanupSpec / SpecLFB):
  - A no-op config script costs 0.29 / 0.29 / 0.50 CPU-s (loading the binary plus gem5's embedded Python).
  - The exit-only binary with the run's se.py flags costs 0.62 / 2.14 / 0.87.
  - `--dump-config= --json-config=` (no config.ini/json written) saves nothing measurable: 0.62 / 2.04 / 0.87.
  - CleanupSpec's extra ~1.8 s is in startup/teardown, not simulation (`host_seconds` 0.01, 2 instructions simulated). Why this build's se.py setup is ~3.4x InvisiSpec's with nearly the same flags was not investigated.

**How AMuLeT runs many test cases per program.** Sources: AMuLeT c871bdf from `~/trippel/amulet3_src/amulet-c871bdf6d143.tar.gz` (read in place), and the gem5 trees `~/trippel/defense_src/amulet-*_AE-v1.1` (CleanupSpec unless noted). The key lines were re-read for this section.
- **One gem5 process per program, not per test case.** `src/executor.py:663-666`: every `trace_test_case` (one program, all its inputs) calls `ipc.restart_gem5`. `src/ipc_orchestration.py:113-126` kills the previous process and keeps `gem5_ipc_parallelism` = 4 processes pre-started. That pool hides startup latency; it does not split the inputs, which run one after another on the active process.
- **gem5 runs AMuLeT's own config.** `configs/revizor/ipc.py` (which imports se.py, so the full Ruby system is still built) calls `m5.instantiate()` once (`:122`) and runs a first `m5.simulate` "to set things up" (`:124`). Then, for each input: `while system.ipc.prepareNext(): m5.simulate(...)` (`:126-141`), with stdout cut at `--- RESET ---`.
- **A C++ SimObject swaps code and data inside the running simulation**, talking to Python over a UNIX socket (`src/revizor_ipc/revizor_ipc.cc`):
  - `loadTestCase` writes the program, at most 512 bytes (`:25`), straight into simulated DRAM at the `code` symbol, NOP-padded (`:270-289`).
  - `traceTestCase` memcpys each input's sandbox and register values (`:304-327`).
  - After the input, `serializeAllCachesToString` (`:370`) returns the cache tags as the observation. No debug-flag trace is written.
- **Reset between inputs is a prologue inside the simulated program** (`configs/revizor/ipc_base_x86.s:37-88`): m5exit, restore rsp, then a defense-specific cache step, then load the registers and FLAGS.
  - CleanupSpec: clflush every sandbox line, then touch every sandbox page to warm the TLB.
  - InvisiSpec: an eviction-set thrash instead (subagent report).
  - SpecLFB: no flush or prime in the asm; only `memWriteback` in C++ (subagent report).
  - Not reset: L1I, L2, the branch predictor (reset only when it is part of the observation), the pipeline and other non-sandbox state. That state carries over from input to input, which is why Revizor's priming stays on.
- **What is amortized.**
  - Per program: gem5 exec, config and Ruby instantiation, one assembly of the program.
  - Per input: socket transfer, the prologue plus the test, the tag dump, TOML parsing in Python.
  - So AMuLeT's 0.015-0.076 CPU-s per test case is essentially simulation plus a tag dump. That is the same order as SimSpect's 0.06-0.08 CPU-s of actual simulation.

**Options** (savings are CPU-s per test on an idle node, InvisiSpec / CleanupSpec / SpecLFB, from the table):

| idea | saving | preserves the exact per-test conditions? | effort | risk |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1. Cache the resolve-hook detection: per (path, size, mtime), or computed once in `pipeline.py` and passed in an env var | 0.62 / 0.65 / 0.41 | yes: same boolean, nothing in the gem5 command changes | ~10 lines in `gem5_common.py` | none |
| 2. One routed trace read: read and unglue once; each parser sees only the line class its patterns can match (every pattern requires that class's literal substring) | most of 1.81 / 1.26 / 1.32 (prototype) | yes: same parser code; 60/60 identical records | ~60 lines | low (regression check) |
| 3. Persistent Python worker: firstdet/testrun call `process_one` in-process instead of `python3 pipeline.py` → `python3 check_footprint.py` per test | 0.37 / 0.19 / ~0.15 | yes: same per-test steps and fresh temp dir; the only per-process state is caches (`_force_support`) | moderate (driver change; keep per-test rusage) | low |
| **1+2+3 together (prototype)** | **per test 1.25 / 1.88 / 1.41 (3.0x / 2.0x / 2.0x)**; 1+2 alone ≈ 1.6 / 2.1 / 1.5 | yes | | |
| 4. Build binaries at generation time | ≤ 0.05-0.1 (`as`/`ld`; `objdump`/`nm` stay per test unless cached; cache setup rewrites the .s, so one binary per setup) | yes if byte-identical | moderate | low; small win |
| 5. Drop the Commit flag for `check_footprint` | ~0.06-0.1 of gem5 time, plus parsing | **no**: the retire fallback fires in 14% of InvisiSpec hits | small | changes the checker's input (TC-3) |
| 6. Trace only a window (`--debug-start`) | unknown | **no**: the checker replays events from the start, and the window is known only after the run | | changes semantics |
| 7. gem5 checkpoint after init, restore per test | ~0: restoring still instantiates every SimObject (the startup cost), and Ruby cannot restore cache contents without its warm-up trace | — | — | no gain |
| 8. Several tests per gem5 process (other programs, both branch modes of one program, or several timing points), AMuLeT-style | gem5 startup 0.36 / 1.14 / 0.48 plus the per-test harness | **no, unless the reset is complete**: L1I, L2, BP, TLB and pipeline carry over; `--branch-ann-file`, `--fnc-commit-stall-*` and the stalls are read at startup per binary. Needs a C++ hook in each gem5 tree (load code and annotation per test, reset) | large, per build | changes what a hit means (TC-2) |
| 9. Faster parsing beyond routing (fewer regexes, compiled) | the rest of parsing, ~0.2-0.3 | yes | moderate | low; diminishing returns |

**Prototype result (`fast_worker.py`, options 1+2+3; cafe2 02:42-02:50 UTC, same 20 tests per build).** In one persistent process, each test ran first through the fast path and then through the unpatched production functions. Before each unpatched run the binary-scan cache was cleared, so it paid the scan the way a fresh checker process does. Both runs used the same load, interleaved per test.
- **Records:** 60/60 identical, key by key (minus `workdir`). That covers `issued_in_window`, `leak`, `terms_fired`, `criteria`, `validity`, every tick, the force plan and the cache-setup info. Fast-path verdicts equal TIME-1's on 60/60.
- **In-process CPU, fast ÷ production:** 0.375 / 0.531 / 0.516. Means on the loaded node:
  - InvisiSpec: warm 1.09 vs 2.90, full 3.19 vs 8.49.
  - CleanupSpec: warm 2.41 vs 4.32, cold 3.06 vs 6.49, full 3.49 vs 6.30.
  - SpecLFB: warm 1.18 vs 2.53, cold 2.05 vs 4.36, full 2.90 vs 5.16.
- **Not proven:** that a persistent process gives the same full records as fresh per-test processes. Only the verdicts were compared to TIME-1 for that (60/60). A production version should run a record-by-record regression against a stored campaign (cf. `STAGE3_gem5/bugs/_regress.py`).

**What it would do to CPU per detection.** This is an estimate, not a measurement. It takes the TIME-1 table above and scales the SimSpect side by the fast-path per-test cost (multiple = AMuLeT ÷ SimSpect, execution only / with 0.93 CPU-s per test of generation):

| bug | today | with the fast path |
|---|---|---|
| A-UV1 | 34x / 27x | ~100x / ~57x |
| A-UV3 | 0.55x / 0.44x | ~1.1x / ~0.73x |
| A-UV4 | 3.4x / 2.7x | ~6.8x / ~4.5x |
| A-UV6 | 6.7x / 5.0x | ~13x / ~7.9x |
| jmp shadow | 13x / 9.8x | ~26x / ~15.5x |
| A-UV5 | bound only (K = 0) | unchanged |

After the fast path, generation (0.93 CPU-s per test) becomes the largest single SimSpect cost. Quote nothing from this table before an idle-node re-time (TC-4).

**Recommendation.**
- **Adopt options 1-3** (TC-1). They are harness-only: same binaries, same gem5 command lines, same checker code, identical records. They make a test about 2-3x cheaper.
- **Re-time the SimSpect half of TIME-1 with them** before changing any paper number (TC-4).
- **Keep the Commit flag** (TC-3).
- **Skip 6 and 7.** Do 4 only if it comes for free.
- **Treat AMuLeT-style batching (8) as a research-scope decision** (TC-2). It is the only way under the ~0.7-1.5 CPU-s gem5 floor, but every test would then start from the previous test's microarchitectural state unless a full reset is built and proven. My recommendation is not before the deadline. If pursued, prototype it on one build and accept it only if its verdicts equal fresh-process runs on a sample.

### Fast path adopted (opt-in, agent fastpath, 2026-11-16)

Anna (2026-11-16): "adopt this fast path asap. dont do batching. just do the fast path. as long as you are certain it wont break anything." Options 1-3 above are now production code behind one run-config key, `gem5.fast_path: true`. The default is off, and absent or `false` means the production path, unchanged. Runbook for the experimentalist (turn it on, check equivalence, re-time, fall back): `wiki/state/COMMANDS.md` "Fast path (opt-in, 2026-11-16)". Decisions: TC-4 (re-time) and TC-5 (which campaigns use it) in `URGENT_TODO.md`.

**Code.** Every change is gated by the key.
- `STAGE3_gem5/gem5_fastpath.py` (new): options 1 and 2. `gem5_common` installs it only when `SIMSPECT_FAST_PATH` is set. Option 1 caches the hook scan per binary (real path, size, mtime, inode), in memory, for the life of the process. Option 2 does a routed single read inside `process_one` only; the cache is thread-local and re-validated on every parser call. Any trace over 512 MB, or any failing read, goes back to the production read for that test, so the production parsers raise exactly what they always raised.
- `STAGE3_gem5/timing/fastpath_worker.py` (new): option 3. `timing_driver.run_one` (and through it `firstdet_stream.py` and TIME-1) hands each test's unchanged `pipeline.py gem5` command line, environment and log file to a persistent worker. The worker runs `pipeline.main()` and the checker script (as `__main__`) in-process. There is one pool per checker script, because `gem5_common` reads its settings at import. A checker batch whose `SIMSPECT_*` env or script differs from the worker's runs as a real subprocess (`exec_path: fast_partial`). If a worker fails, the test is rerun the production way (`exec_path: production`).
- `gem5_common.py`: a 14-line tail that installs the above when the env var is set.
- `pipeline.py`: `_build_gem5_env` maps the key to `SIMSPECT_FAST_PATH=1`; a non-bool value is refused, and an absent key drops any stray env var. `_run_checker_batch` points the fast-path log next to the batch output.
- `timing_driver.py`: the fast branch. The production subprocess block is unchanged (only indented) and is also the per-test fallback.
- `firstdet_stream.py`, `testrun.py`: log lines, the log location and a manifest key, written only when the key is on.
- Unchanged: gem5 and its hooks, debug flags (Commit kept, TC-3), cache setup, branch forces, stalls, every checker's decision code. gem5 still runs once per test (TC-2).

**The default path is unchanged; how this was verified.**
1. With the key absent, `gem5_common` never imports `gem5_fastpath`, and every function is the original object (checked in-process).
2. Old vs new `_build_gem5_env` give identical checker environments for all 226 run configs in the repo.
3. The new code's production path reproduces the live big-corpus campaigns, which ran the pre-fast-path code (md5-identical to the Mac tree before this change), on every shared test. `fastpath_equiv.py stored`, with only the code-root path masked inside `criterion_path`:
   - T1g: 52/52 rows and 52/52 hit records.
   - T2g: 338/338 rows and 49/49 hit records.
   - T3g: 45/45 rows and 26/26 hit records.
   - CleanupSpec on the extra corpus: 16/16 rows and 3/3 hit records.
   - E2w: 324/324 hit and status values against the running shards.

   The fast path matches the same stored records.

**Equivalence results** (cafe3 `gem5_dev:/tests/fastpath_20261116/`, launcher `STAGE3_gem5/timing/fastpath_equiv_run_20261116.sh`, nice 19; SPT on cafe2 in the same dir name). The `driver` check runs options 1+2+3. For each test it compares the full checker record(s) key by key (minus the temp `workdir` path), the `window-results.json` record, the sweep sidecar and the `results.jsonl` row minus timing fields. Three normalizations are applied, each needed for two PRODUCTION runs too: random tempfile directory names, each side's own output dir, and glibc-backtrace frame addresses in a crashing gem5's stderr (ASLR).

| check | build (checkers) | tests | identical | hits in the set |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| driver | InvisiSpec T1g (check_footprint) | 324 (300 seeded random + 24 known: UV1, UV1_transient, other_*) | 324 | 324 (UV1 154) |
| driver | CleanupSpec T2g | 338 (300 + 38 known: UV3, UV4, CS1, EM_*, UV3_other_actor, padding, unattributed) | 338 | 49 (UV3 16, UV4 5, CS1 4) |
| driver | CleanupSpec T2g, extra corpus | 21 (A-UV5 inst-061878 not_taken + 20) | 21 | 3 (UV5 1: `over_clean` on both) |
| driver | SpecLFB T3g | 317 (300 + 18 known: UV6, jmp shadow, walk order, replayed) | 317 | 153 (UV6 85, jmp shadow 47, walk order 48) |
| driver | ReCon-STT E2w, gem5-recon-modded (check_ld, check_br) | 324 (300 + 24 known ld / br_x hits) | 324 | 212 (ld 143, br_x 69) |
| driver | STT E3, stt-master (check_ld, check_br) | 200 | 200 | 36 (S1_xmm4 in classify) |
| driver | SPT-Fence, gem5-spt, `se_exp.py` delay 1 (check_ld, check_br) | 80 (seeded random; cafe2) | 80 | 9 (ld 6, br_x 3) |
| driver, synthetic errors | T2g + E2w | 32: assembler failure, `.ann.json` without `xmit.kind` (pipeline crash, status `missing`), without the transmitter address (`no xmit x86 PC`), unmapped load (gem5 panic, or a cache-setup remap error) | 32 | 0 |
| driver, gem5 timeouts (`SIMSPECT_GEM5_TIMEOUT_S=0`) | T2g + E2w | 12 | 12 | 0 |
| A/A control (production twice) | T2g; T2g + E2w error cases | 58; 32 | 58; 32 | 40; 0 |
| batch (`pipeline.py gem5` / `testrun.py` path: options 1+2, checker threads, jobs 8) | T2g, E2w, T1g, T3g | 604 (188 + 174 + 124 + 118, each 100-150 random + known hits) | 604 | 342 (45 / 105 / 124 / 68) |
| parsers, offline | stored big-corpus hit traces (150 each of T1g/T2g/T3g) + 6 synthetic edge cases each | 450 traces + 18 edge cases | all | n/a |
| hit attribution: `bugs/classify.py footprint` on each side, then `_regress.py classes` | T1g 324, T2g 49 (+ RMW classifier: UV3 16, `cs_evidence` 49 keys), extra-corpus T2g 3, T3g 153, E2w 212, E3 36 | | 0 changed labels, 0 changed causes | |

The error-case A/A control showed why the two maskings are needed. Before them, production vs production differed on exactly the same 14 tests and fields as production vs fast: `stall_points_error` embeds the side's own work dir, and the gem5 panic message ends in backtrace addresses. `check_other` is covered only by construction (it reads `by_pc` only, like `check_ld`): no corpus in use has an `other_x` transmitter (E2w: 30,368 tests, kinds `ld` / `br_x` only). In 1,648 driver tests (with SPT), every fast test ran fully in the worker (`exec_path: fast`): no `fast_partial`, no fallback, no worker error. The 36 tests logged with "production read" are the error cases, where gem5 wrote no trace.

**Per-test CPU, both paths on the same tests, same node, same time** (cafe3, load 40-58 on 64 hardware threads, nice 19, 8 threads per check; each test ran production then fast, interleaved). Mean `total_cpu_s` per test is the whole per-test tree: build, gem5, checker and, for production, both interpreters.

| build | production | fast | fast ÷ production | warm | cold | full |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| InvisiSpec T1g | 6.16 | 2.25 | 0.37 | 3.34 → 1.21 | (none: clflush no-op) | 9.27 → 3.40 |
| CleanupSpec T2g | 6.27 | 3.10 | 0.49 | 4.68 → 2.41 | 6.67 → 3.22 | 7.13 → 3.52 |
| SpecLFB T3g | 4.55 | 2.19 | 0.48 | 2.68 → 1.26 | 4.86 → 1.97 | 5.75 → 3.10 |
| ReCon-STT E2w | 2.79 | 1.26 | 0.45 | (warm only) | | |
| STT E3 | 6.53 | 2.41 | 0.37 | 3.18 → 1.13 | 5.03 → 1.80 | 11.21 → 4.22 |
| SPT-Fence (cafe2, load ~51) | 7.44 | 2.94 | 0.40 | 3.66 → 1.31 | (none: SPT panics on clflush) | 11.23 → 4.58 |

- **Worker startup:** about 0.07 CPU-s per worker (8 workers ≈ 0.6 CPU-s per check), i.e. about 0.002 CPU-s per test, recorded in `fastpath_workers.jsonl`.
- **The cached hook scan:** 0.06 s, once per worker.
- **Batch path:** only options 1 and 2 apply, since interpreters and scans were already amortized there. CPU of the whole `pipeline.py gem5` call, fast ÷ production: CleanupSpec 0.60 (904 → 545 CPU-s, 188 tests), ReCon-STT 0.63 (339 → 215, 174), InvisiSpec 0.47 (577 → 273, 124), SpecLFB 0.59 (452 → 266, 118). Every test in the batch checks was logged `path=fast` in `fastpath.log`, from 16-32 checker threads, so the thread-local routing was exercised.
- **What this means for an idle node:** applied to TIME-1's idle production means (3.69 / 3.74 / 2.78), these ratios give about 1.35 / 1.85 / 1.34 CPU-s per test for InvisiSpec / CleanupSpec / SpecLFB. The prototype estimated 1.25 / 1.88 / 1.41. This is an estimate under load, not a measurement: the idle re-time is TC-4, runbook in COMMANDS.md (`STAGE3_gem5/timing/time1_20261115/retime_fast.sh`).

**Left for the experimentalist.**
1. TC-4: the idle re-time with `retime_fast.sh`. It also checks the 3,000 fast rows against a production rerun.
2. Running the equivalence check on any new config or build before turning the key on there.
3. Nothing on the nodes is left running. The node dirs (cafe3 856 MB, cafe2 small) are input copies plus outputs; delete them when no longer needed.

### 9. Non-minimal footprint corpus (nonmin, 2026-11-10)

Anna's TODO (URGENT_TODO, 2026-11-10): "a non-minimal version later, so the generator is not restricted from any shape (e.g. no RR/RS minimality, only the connectivity rule and the Stage-1 core pin), as its own corpus next to the minimal one." Agent nonmin built it for the footprint model and ran the same first-detection protocol as part 7. **Answer: dropping minimality does not change the picture.** The minimum stays UV5. With the same estimator on both sides it is 23.7x on the non-minimal corpus against 17.8x on the minimal one (6 against 4 UV5 hits; the intervals overlap almost completely). UV3 and UV4 become rarer per test (0.85x and 0.61x the minimal rate). No new known-bug class appeared. One new unexplained CleanupSpec hit class needs a look: one program, an L2 line re-filled after its cleanup. Generation is cheaper: 1.84 against 3.0 CPU-s per test. Decisions: `URGENT_TODO.md` NM-1..NM-4.

**Model.** `STAGE1_alloy/models/proposed/ultimate/loosepin/Footprint_6_u_lpN.als` is a NEW, UNAPPROVED file built by `make_loosepin.py --base ../Footprint_6_u.als --n 8 --operands 16 --intbits 6 --minimality none --committed required --brcap 2 --pin core --flagcanon`. It shares production `Footprint_6_u_lpP.als`'s skeleton, bound `but 8`, prefix IX, committed region, no-junk rule `lp_connected` and path-style Stage-1 core pin (`seed_core` appended per core). The defense block is byte-identical to `_lpP` (checked), and `check_bound_consistency.py` passes. Three things differ from `_lpP`. (1) `gen_useful_litmus` is the leak only: `not secure_speculation_scheme_p[no_p]`, with no RR, no RS and no boundary RC+RR clause (`--minimality none`, new). (2) `fact lp_brcap`: at most 2 unresolved branches (NM-1). (3) `fact lp_flagcanon` [lp-9, new]: every non-branch instruction is resolved (NM-2). Under this contract a non-branch's resolved bit is inert: `has_unresolved_brs_p` reads branch resolution only, and Stage 2 reads `resolved` only for branches (and stores in stl_bypass mode). Without RR the bit would be free, and each program would appear up to 2^k times. The minimal corpus already has 0 programs with an unresolved non-branch (`nonmin_fp.py flags`, 0 of 25,116), so this is exactly the production convention. `make_loosepin.py` still regenerates `Footprint_6_u_lpPc.als` byte-identically. `stage15lp.py --relax-nonbranch-resolved` (new) drops a core's `not in resolved` literal for non-branch core instructions. Here it relaxed 0 literals, because no core has one.

Stage 1 reused regen-lp's 1,411 cores (363 distinct at the pin level, the same `cores.json` dedup). `stage15lp.py` ran with 32 slots, 25 instances / 300 s per job, seeded random order (walk p 0.05), queue shuffle 20261110, spread 2, target 26,500. `stream_fp.py` ran recipe v3 (`stage2_fv3.jsonc`, only `testset_dir` and the asm thread count changed), then RMW `--select eligible` p 0.5, then jmp uniform p 0.5, with stems from `inst-3000001`. Dedup against the minimal corpus uses `--seen-from` with the flag-normalized fingerprints of `Footprint_6_u_core_lpP_fv3` (`nonmin_fp.py seen`; normalized = raw for both corpora). Stage 1.5 finished at **27,095 distinct programs** after 1,210 jobs in 1,864 s. The same 6 cores are EXHAUSTED at 0 as in production. Jobs cap in about 35 s against 300 s under production minimality, so the search is about 9x faster. **Overlap with the minimal corpus: 625 programs (2.3%)**, which were dropped. Corpus `testsets_twostage/Footprint_6_u_core_lpN_fv3/` (cafe1; copies on cafe2 and cafe3): **26,470 programs, 52,940 tests, 0 Stage-2 failures**, 32 batches, `rmw_stems.tsv` 21,224 lines, `jmp_stems.tsv` 26,403 lines. - **Reproduction. Verdict: all 8 records reproduce exactly, 2 of 2 times each.** The 8 records are the 6 UV5 hits plus inst-3002295 in both modes. Each rerun has the same hit, terms, cache setup, timing point, lc and fnc retire ticks, and offending items. The md5 of the whole `O3PipeView:simspect` hook-event stream is also identical to the campaign trace, even though the campaign ran on cafe3/cafe2 and the reruns on cafe1 (for example taken inst-3002295 `1f26fc8bd3`, 4,786 events, in all three runs).
- **UV5. Verdict: all 6 are real UV5 (CleanupSpec over-cleaning), the known AMuLeT bug.** Every record has the full signature on L1D and L2: `over_clean`, `claimed_by_older` 1, `invalidated` 1, `d_install` -1. Every record is also `inconsistent` (the DG-3 window caveat, as in the minimal run). In the trace, S (the filler) never retires, and U (the claimer) is older than the first forced branch and retires. U hits S's E install, then S's `Inv_CLEANUP st=E` removes the line from L1D and L2 (`Cleanup_Invalidate_clean`):
  - taken inst-3020047, line 0x7580: S = seq 3158, pc 0x404473 `ld rcx,[rdx+r13]`, fills L1D at 17498500. U = seq 3144, pc 0x40443c `ld r8,[r15+r13]`, hits at 17499000 and retires at 17573500. Cleanup: L1D cleanup_inv at 22427500, L2 cleanup_inv at 22441500.
  - not_taken inst-3020047: the same S/U (S at pc 0x404469). U hits at 17499500. Cleanup: L1D at 22420500, L2 at 22435000.
  - taken inst-3025824, line 0x75c0: S = seq 3140, pc 0x404438 `ld rcx,[rax+r14]`, fills at 17423000. U = seq 3121, pc 0x4043fb `ldfp xmm9_low,[r8+r14]` (FP-routed, as in TARGETING_AUDIT S3), hits at 17456500 and retires at 17582000. Cleanup: L1D at 22587000, L2 at 22601000.
  - not_taken inst-3025824: the same S/U. Cleanup: L1D at 22587500, L2 at 22601500.
  - not_taken inst-3005867, line 0x7580: S = seq 3147, pc 0x404432, fills at 17424000. U = seq 3131, pc 0x404411 `ld r13,[r12+r14]`, hits at 17425000 and retires at 17505000; here U is the first non-committed instruction, not the lc. Cleanup: L1D at 19858000, L2 at 19872500.
  - not_taken inst-3007528, line 0x7580: S = seq 3145, pc 0x404438, fills at 17431500. U = seq 3127, pc 0x404404 `ld r8,[r8+rsi]`, hits at 17432500 and retires at 17514000 (U is the lc). Cleanup: L1D at 22352500, L2 at 22367000.
  - As in DG-6, all six S/U pairs use a constant stack line (0x7580/0x75c0 = 0x7fffffffe580/e5c0).
- **NM-4, inst-3002295. Verdict: not a new gem5 bug. The leak is the known CS-EM path (`EM_rmw`: the test's own squashed RMW upgrades a squashed load's E install to M, so the load's cleanup is skipped). It shows up as `unattributed` because of a classifier gap, set off by a quirk in how the checker counts per line.** It is not UV3 (no `why=nohitinfo` skip), not UV4 (no `why=split`), not UV5 (no claim, no cleanup_inv on the leaking episode), and not H-PAD (the upgrader's pc is the xmit_pc). It is not a setup artifact either: the cold flush removed 0x7580 at 4875500. Trace for taken mode (`rerun_1`, identical to the campaign):
  - Episode 1, in W. W = (lc_retire 17440500, fnc_retire 17455000). At 17438500 the lc store, seq 3142 at pc 0x404429 `movq %r9,16(%rdi)`, raises ROB squash sq=3. Its younger fnc load, seq 3143 at pc 0x40442d `ld rax,[rdi+0x10]`, reads the same address, so this looks like a memory-order replay. The squash kills the first speculative pass. That pass's load S1, seq 3159 at pc 0x40445c `ldfp xmm10_low,[r9+rdi]` (branch target pc6), had filled L2 0x7580 at 17442500. Its cleanup runs correctly: `L1D:cleanup_drop` (in flight) at 17441500, then `L2:cleanup_inv … Cleanup_Invalidate_clean` at 17445500. This episode leaves nothing behind.
  - Episode 2, after W. The branch is re-fetched as seq 3179 at pc 0x404440 (forced taken at 17441500, resolve held 2500 cycles). The same load runs again as S2, seq 3185 at pc 0x40445c. S2 fills L2 at 17514000 and L1D (E) at 17585500. The RMW transmitter, seq 3198 at pc 0x404485 = xmit_pc `OR_M_I ldst`, hits L1D at 17590000 `st=E nst=M req=st`. The branch resolves at 18705000 and raises squash sq=5. S2's cleanup is then skipped: `L1D:cleanup_skip … ev=Inv_CLEANUP st=M nst=M` at 18706500. Neither S2 nor 3198 retires, and the line stays in L1D and L2. not_taken mode is the same chain: S1 seq 3159, L2 cleanup_inv at 17446000; S2 seq 3192 fills at 17514500/17590500; the RMW seq 3205 at pc 0x404477 does E->M at 17595000; the skip at st=M comes at 18707000.
  - Why it is `unattributed`. `check_footprint.py` (per-line loop, about :352-415) counts every actor install on a line across the whole run. It marks the line in-window if any one of those installs is in W. So the L2 item adds up S1's in-window fill, S1's cleanup_inv and S2's out-of-window fill (installed 2, invalidated 1, d_install 1, `footprint_in_window` true). The L1D item has only S2's fill, so it goes to `under_clean_outside_window`. `cleanupspec_skip_evidence.py judge()` (:141-149) gives `EM_upgrade_squashed` only when every event of the item is a fill by one actor. Here the L2 item mixes two actors and a cleanup_inv, so it falls through to "term under_clean events [fill, cleanup_inv, fill]". If S2's fill alone were judged, the existing rule would match: one Inv_CLEANUP st=M skip by S2, and one squashed E->M upgrader at the xmit_pc of an RMW stem (`EM_rmw`).
  - Remaining ambiguity: which label to give it. The record counts as in-window only because of the episode that was cleaned correctly. Under a strict per-episode window, the checker should not have flagged it in W at all, which would make it a checker artifact. The leaking episode itself sits in the branch's real speculative window, and it is the known CS-EM defect, which would make it a classifier gap. Either way it is neither a new candidate nor a target bug, and no target K changes (it was never counted). No review md was written.
- **CPU per test (cafe1, same method as part 8 check 6).** The first 1,000 tests of the seed order per defense, T1g/T2g/T3g nonmin configs, 3 rows x 2 workers, 3,000 of 3,000 `ok`. **Caveat:** the node was quiet (load about 6 of 64) only until about 19:30 UTC. After that another session's `firstdet_final` checker jobs held the load at about 20-26 of 64. The first 500 tests per row (quiet period) give a total of InvisiSpec 3.52, CleanupSpec 3.47, SpecLFB 2.55 CPU-s per test. That is the same as the minimal corpus's dedicated numbers (3.60 / 3.49 / 2.62). All 1,000 tests give a total / gem5-only of InvisiSpec 3.66 / 0.70 (full 5.31, warm 2.12), CleanupSpec 3.61 / 1.20 (cold 3.96, full 4.20, warm 2.75), and SpecLFB 2.69 / 0.69. With the 1.84 CPU-s per test of generation, the CPU multiples (all-1,000 means; AMuLeT pooled CPU-s from part 8; execution only in brackets) are: UV1 24.3x (36.6x); **UV3 0.60x (0.91x)**; UV4 8.2x (12.4x); **UV5 0.15x (0.23x)**; UV6 5.6x (9.5x); jmp 12.3x (20.6x). The minimum on CPU is still UV5: AMuLeT is about 7x cheaper, against about 10x on the minimal corpus (0.094x). Two things drive the difference: generation is cheaper (1.84 against 3.0 CPU-s per test) and the estimator is (N+1)/(K+1) with 6 hits. Summary: `nm_diligence/cpu/summary.txt`.

**Diligence on the 100% leftovers (2026-11-10 23:30 UTC onward; every leftover reproduced exactly as its run config did, `TMPDIR=/tmp`-style short temp dirs, all reproduced):**

- **E3/E4 S1-by-pattern (7 stems):** direct test: `%xmm4` renamed to an unused `%xmm0-7` register (same encoding length, `.text` sizes identical, ann unchanged; testset `testsets_twostage/STT_6_u_core_lpP_fv3__s1rename`), rerun on E3 and E4, both modes: **0 of 28 hit** (all held; every original hits). So all 7 (inst-009888, 013526, 018038, 018549, 021390, 024022, 025271) are **S1_xmm4, confirmed**; the static slice tool misses `idivq`'s implicit operands. E3/E4 100%: every gated hit is S1_xmm4, nothing unexplained.
- **E2 leftovers (9 stems):** faithful GPR twin with the corpus test's own RMW rewrite and jmp insert (`make_faithful_twin.sh`, 18/18 tests matched; testset `STT_6_u_core_lpP_fv3_ftwin_e2left`). Twin held for inst-009573, 022875, 022928, 025846 (br_x), inst-012370 (ld) and inst-011701 not_taken: the hit needs the FP routing, and each has an SSE load and an `idivq` (the e2-losttaint idiv-divisor case) → **R-SA-13 (FP elsewhere)**. inst-020519, inst-020566 (RMW, both modes) and inst-011701 taken (RMW): main run `isTainted=0` (R-SA-13); twin hit is the RMW's load micro-op `isTainted=1 isSpeculative=1`, no forward, no ready → **R-SA-13 in the main run, check_ld RMW artifact in the twin**. inst-012910 (br_x, both modes, no SSE load, no idiv): twin hits; trace (`Commit,DelayUnit,Rename`) shows the transmitter branch inserted into the delay unit, released "no longer tainted" at 2927000 when the older pre-window branch's load commits, and resolving mispredicted at 2931000 while the window branch is held to 4178000 → **R-SA-8 (verified by trace)**.
- **E2 R-SA-8 candidates (27 tests):** `rsa8_trace.py` (delay-unit signature: inserted, released before the held window branch fires, transmitter resolves mispredicted before it) on the twins: **26 / 27 carry the R-SA-8 signature**. The exception, **inst-008176 mispredict_taken**, is **R-SA-8 too (E2LT-2, mov false dependency), in a second shape the signature tool missed** (resolved 2026-11-11 00:20; first written here as ambiguous). The Rename trace shows the pc5 `movq %rdi,%rax` (sn 63) reading the old `%rax` (phys 51, written by the pc1 load sn 31, which is store-shadow speculative and forwarded) as a merge source next to `%rdi` (phys 79, the pc4 window load's chain). `getOldestTaint` keeps only sn 31 as the source, sn 31 turns non-speculative when the pc0/pc2 store addresses resolve, the taint is freed, and the transmitter branch (renamed at 187000 after an i-cache gap) is never tainted, so it is never inserted into the delay unit and resolves mispredicted at 189000, 2.4M ticks before the window branch fires (2635500). Shape B ("freed before the branch was ready") vs shape A ("inserted, then released early"). The forwarding from store idx 6 is incidental. Traces: cafe2 `loosepin_regen/bugwork/rsa8/`.
- **E6 leftovers (27 stems, faithful twins, 54 / 54 tests matched; testset `Recon_6_u_core_lpP_fv3_ftwin_e6left`):** br_x without a twin (inst-002667, 012449, 012922, 019590, 022175): twin held, each has an SSE load and an `idivq` → **R-SA-13 (FP elsewhere)**. ld held in the twin (inst-002260, 007152, 007705, 008235, 014710, 019400, 025586) → **R-SA-13 (FP elsewhere)**. ld with a twin hit (15, all RMW): inst-008152, 009805 twin carries the A2 signature → **R-SA-13 main / A2 twin**; inst-000442, 003716, 005212, 007833 (reveal 1 but tainted), 008205, 013249, 015036, 016747, 020046, 022333, 023390, 023690 twin RMW load micro-op `isTainted=1 isSpeculative=1`, no forward → **R-SA-13 main / check_ld RMW artifact twin**; **inst-013366 (both modes) → check_ld artifact, faulting RMW transmitter**: the `orq $0,(%rdi,%r14)` `ldst` micro-op logs `Load [sn:580] not executed from fault` (twin) and `Load [sn:584] not executed from fault` (main corpus), with no STL_CHECK and no memory request, yet check_ld scores it (traces cafe2 `loosepin_regen/bugwork/trace_013366{,_main}/`). Same family as the E3/E4 RMW `never_executed` false positives, but this one passed the data-obtained gate. DECIDE item below.
- **E6 R-SA-8 candidates (146 tests):** **125 / 146 carry the shape-A signature.** Of the 21 with the branch never inserted, 19 have reveals in the twin → **reveal artifact (E2LT-3) with a branch transmitter**. **inst-022384 (both modes) → R-SA-8, shape B**, found with a direct taint probe (no reveal, `allowlistedTaintsFromTT` 0, no phys reg 16, no squash before the window). Byte-neutral probe loads `movl (%rsp,%reg),%edi`, whose address taint STL_CHECK prints (labels and `.text` size identical, ann unchanged; cafe2 `loosepin_regen/bugwork/probe{,2}_022384/`): `%r9` right after the pc4 window load is tainted (`isTainted=1`, put in the delay unit), but `%rax` right after `movq %r9,%rax` is not. The Rename trace shows that mov reading the old `%rax` (phys 98, pc1 load sn 554, store-shadow speculative) as a merge source, so the oldest-source rule kept sn 554 and freed the taint when its store shadow lifted. (An earlier note in this session called this stem a concretization artifact. That was wrong: the predicate depends on the load through `imulq` and `idivq`.)
- **Net for the four rows at 100%: nothing left unexplained; no new gem5 bug.** Every leftover is R-SA-13, R-SA-8 (E2LT-2, both shapes), the reveal artifact (E2LT-3), A2, or a check_ld artifact (RMW limm, RMW fault). **For Anna:** (1) `rsa8_trace.py` should also accept shape B (taint freed before the branch is ready). Proposed rule: on the twin, the transmitter's chain has a reg-reg `mov` whose old destination was written by an older speculative load. (2) DECIDE: should check_ld drop transmitters whose load micro-op logs `not executed from fault`? Recommendation: yes. The access never left the core, and today's data-obtained gate lets it through.

E6 100% (26,506 stems per mode, cafe2; four twins a-d, recon_stlf evidence on 9,041 keys, reveal check on 751 twin keys; not_taken / taken): br_x 4,193 / 4,317 of 9,793: R-SA-13 2,024 / 2,094 (twin) + 2,078 / 2,140 (static), R-SA-8 candidates 77 / 69, FP elsewhere 9 / 9, unexplained without a twin 5 / 5 (inst-012922, 022175, 012449 xmm; inst-002667, 019590 GPR); ld plain 5,256 / 5,251 of 9,578 / 9,575: R-SA-13 1,746 / 1,743 + 2,298 / 2,308, A2 411 / 412 (twin) + 411 / 398 (main-run evidence), R-SA-13 main / R-SA-9 twin 316 / 308, **R-SA-13 main / reveal artifact in twin (E2LT-3) 55 / 66**, FP elsewhere 12 / 9, untainted without a twin 7 / 7; ld RMW 6,589 / 6,591 of 7,135 / 7,138: R-SA-13 static 3,260 / 3,291, check_ld RMW artifact 2,742 / 2,720, A2 573 / 565, gate-disqualified 209 / 210, untainted without a twin 13 / 14, 1 / 1 with no STL_CHECK line in the rerun (inst-013366). Diligence on the 27 leftover stems (faithful twins) and the 146 R-SA-8 candidates (traces): done 2026-11-11, in the diligence list above.

Method at 100%: GPR twins of every gated plain hit stem in four batches (E2 a-d, E6 a-d; the 100% twins run as 8-10 shards with `shard_run.py`), recon_stlf evidence on the main + shard runs (`run_evidence_par.sh`: one process per run in parallel), `reveal_check.py` on the twins, `bucket_eval.py`. JSONs `loosepin_regen/eval_results/{gate,buckets}_<row>_100pct.json`.

**Cache setup (Anna 2026-11-09):** `gem5.cache_setup: random` (per-test hot/cold, chosen from sha256(seed, stem), recorded on every record) for CleanupSpec (E13) and SpecLFB (E14); warm everywhere else; the parity configs (T2, T3) keep AMuLeT's setting (today `warm`; AMuLeT itself differs per build, DECIDE in `URGENT_TODO.md`); not a standard dimension. E13 now runs `--L2_TBEs=3` (was 2): with 2, cold panicked CleanupSpec's cleanup (`L2cache.sm:308`) on 326/590 tests; 3 is the smallest crash-free value measured, and warm hits are unchanged (13/590, same stems, at 2/3/4/8/16). Details: `wiki/design/SIMSPECT_HOOKS.md` "Cache setup strategies".

Paths starting `testsets_latest/`, `runconfigs/fixed_20261030/` and `results/` are in the fixed-harness copy on cafe2, `/tests/simspect_fixed_20261030/` (the run configs are also in this repo, as are `runconfigs/footprint/` and `runconfigs/amulet_parity/`).

- **Confirm new bugs at parity.** Before claiming any NEW footprint bug, re-run its test once at parity (the T1-T3 configs), so a reviewer can't call it an artifact of the shrunk caches. jmp-shadow already reproduced on a pristine build.
- **Even at parity the comparison is not identical, so state this beside every T number.**
  - AMuLeT starts each test with a filled or flushed L1D, uses a 1-page sandbox (128 pages for STT), and decides relationally across 140 inputs.
  - We start each test with a cold cache and decide from one trace.

### Timing results: SimSpect at AMuLeT parity on STT_6_fv2 (timing-amulet, 2026-11-09)

**Setup.** Machine: cafe1 `gem5_dev` (64 cores, dedicated, nothing else running; load about 18 from these runs alone). Testset: two-stage `STT_6_fv2` (cafe2 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/testsets_twostage/STT_6_fv2`, 107,601 (mode, stem) pairs with both `.s` and `.ann.json` when the order was drawn at 11:40 UTC). Configs: `runconfigs/amulet_parity_fv2/T{1..4}_STT_6_fv2.jsonc`, path-only copies of `runconfigs/amulet_parity/` (testset_dir, results_name, instruction_tables). T2c/T3c add one key, `gem5.cache_setup: cold`, because AMuLeT starts CleanupSpec and SpecLFB tests from flushed sandbox lines (`fu-2025-amulet.pdf` p9). `config.ini` of each run matches the AMuLeT column of `wiki/findings/audits/2026-10-28/full_configs.md`: L1D 32768/8, L2 2MB/8, TBEs 256, sys clock 500 ps, DTB 64, `access_backing_store=true`, needsTSO false (true for STT), BiModeBP on SpecLFB.

**Method.** One fixed order for every defense: the sorted (mode, stem) list shuffled with `random.Random(20261109)` (`order_seed20261109.tsv`). One `pipeline.py gem5` call per test, 6 at a time (`STAGE3_gem5/timing/timing_driver.py`). Per test: gem5 CPU = user+sys of the gem5 process (os.wait4 via `STAGE3_gem5/timing/sitecustom/sitecustomize.py`, no source edits); total CPU = the whole pipeline.py subtree (assemble, link, checker, gem5). Hits are attributed by `STAGE3_gem5/timing/classify_hits.py`, which reuses existing rules: UV1 = an in-window `L1D:evict req=specld`; UV4/UV5/CS1 = `cleanupspec_skip_evidence.py` unchanged; UV6/walk-order/jmp-shadow = the SpecLFB triage tags (`speclfb_analyze.py`, vendored from the 2026-11-07 triage). "Tests to first" is the position in the order; "expected" is (N+1)/(K+1) for K detections in N tests. For K=0 the bound is "> N tests".

None is UV1. UV1 needs a spec load to evict a live line, and at 32 kB/8 our tests never fill a set; AMuLeT fills the L1D with lines that conflict with the sandbox before each InvisiSpec test (p9), and `cache_setup` has no such mode (it is refused on InvisiSpec). So T1 measures nothing comparable yet.

InvisiSpec 2.1, CleanupSpec 1.1, SpecLFB 1.6, STT 10371; throughput 630 / 2592 / 2595 / 34 test cases per second. In the code that number is campaign wall time divided by the total violation count of all 100 instances (`src/benchmark.py:267-268`, `avg_wall_time/total_violations`), so it is a rate, not a time to first detection, and it counts violations of any kind. With about one core per instance it is roughly 210 / 110 / 160 / 1.04M core-seconds per violation (derived, not measured). That is the same kind of number as our total CPU-s / K: CleanupSpec UV4 247 (warm) and 224 (cold); SpecLFB UV6 164 (warm) and 3.0 (cold).

### CleanupSpec first detection with RMW fanout (firstdet-cleanupspec, 2026-11-09)

**Setup.** Corpus `STT_6_fv2fp_rmw` (row C1-fv: STT_6_fv2fp with 27,511 of 186,082 tests turned into value-neutral RMW transmitters; the single fanout version for all defenses). Configs `runconfigs/amulet_parity_fv2/T2_STT_6_fv2fp_rmw.jsonc` (warm) and `T2c_STT_6_fv2fp_rmw.jsonc` (cold), copies of T2/T2c with only `results_name` changed; `config.ini` shows L1D 32768/8, L2 2097152/8 (parity). Machine cafe3 `gem5_dev`, **shared** with firstdet-simspect (load about 50/64), 12 workers per run, TMPDIR=/tmp (H-STK). Same method as above: order = sorted (mode, stem) pairs of the new corpus shuffled with `random.Random(20261109)`, N = 4000 (first 4000 of the order, all finished, 0 errors in both runs; 568 of them RMW tests), `timing_driver.py`, then `classify_hits.py --defense cleanupspec`, then the new `STAGE3_gem5/timing/classify_cs_rmw.py`, which keeps UV4/UV5/CS-1 from `cleanupspec_skip_evidence.py` and credits UV3 (fill req=st by a never-retired actor + `cleanup_skip why=nohitinfo`, the `uv3_rmw_evidence.py` rule) and E→M only when the actor/upgrader pc is the xmit pc of an RMW stem. **Padding rule (Anna 2026-11-09):** when the actor is the H-PAD padding RMW (ADD_M_R after the xmit; the source corpus has no memory-destination ALU op), the hit goes to an excluded bucket and never counts. Launcher `STAGE3_gem5/timing/firstdet_cs_launch.sh`.

**Which cache state is parity: cold.** AMuLeT's CleanupSpec AE assembles `configs/revizor/ipc_base_x86.s` for its runs (`configs/revizor/ipc.py:49,98-103`), and that program clflushes every sandbox line before each input (`ipc_base_x86.s:49-57`), then loads one word per sandbox page for the TLB (`:59-67`); no L1D thrash (cafe2 `/work/amulet/amulet-gem5-CleanupSpec_AE-v1.1`). This matches the paper (p9). The older "no reset" note is true only of the gem5-side `revizor_ipc.cc`. Difference left: AMuLeT's TLB loop re-warms the first line of each sandbox page; our cold flushes the whole test frame.

- Harness errors: 0. The 41 T2c errors on STT_6_fv2 ("expected 1 prologue mfence, found 2") were the fanout-v2 OTB tests' second prologue barrier; fixed in `cache_setup.find_barrier` (`wiki/design/SIMSPECT_HOOKS.md` "Two-barrier fix").

**T5 plan (blocked; Anna's OK needed).** AMuLeT's fuzzer needs Python 3.11 with `unicorn pyyaml types-pyyaml numpy toml iced-x86 protobuf==3.20 pydot xxhash` (`docker/docker_invisispec/entrypoint.sh`). Neither cafe image has them, and downloading a standalone Python + those wheels into `/tests/.../timing/amulet_env` was refused by the permission classifier as external code. Steps once allowed, all on cafe1:
1. Copy `/work/amulet-repo` (289 MB) from cafe2.
2. Install Python 3.11 + the packages above into `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/timing/amulet_env` (no system change).
3. Copy the four YAMLs that `src/benchmark_all.sh:21-35` names and point `gem5_location`, `gem5_binary` and `gem5_se` at `/work/amulet/amulet-gem5-<D>_AE-v1.1` (build `X86_MESI_Two_Level` for IS/CS, `X86` for SpecLFB/STT). These binaries contain RevizorIPC (checked with grep), but they also carry SimSpect's print-only hooks; either accept that (hooks are off without debug flags) or rebuild pristine AE trees (hours).
4. From `src/`, run `benchmark.py -i 70 -n 200 -r 60 -c <yaml> --extra-args=<benchmark_all.sh args> -p <D> -o benchmark-out-<D>`, under a wrapper that records `RUSAGE_CHILDREN`. Report total CPU-s / violations (the same kind as our CPU-s / K) and the per-instance "First Violation (s)" column (about CPU-s to first detection when there is one core per instance).
5. Effort: about 1 h of setup once downloads are allowed. Runs take about 20-75 min each for IS/CS/SpecLFB (paper campaign times 18 min to 1 h 14 min at 100 instances). STT takes about 23 h (Table 4) and is optional.

### T5: AMuLeT on cafe1 (firstdet-amulet, 2026-11-09)

**Unblocked without downloads.** The June AMuLeT run used `/opt/amulet-venv` (Python 3.11.8 from `/opt/python`, with unicorn 2.1.4, protobuf 3.20.0, iced-x86, numpy, PyYAML, toml, pydot, xxhash). cafe1's container is a clone of cafe2's, so the venv was already on cafe1; nothing was installed. `/work/amulet-repo` (c871bdf) was copied from cafe2 to cafe1 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/timing/amulet_t5/amulet-repo`.

**Setup.** cafe1 `gem5_dev`, 64 cores; nothing else was simulating (other sessions' idle watchers only; load about 35-42 from these runs). Builds: `/work/amulet/amulet-gem5-<D>_AE-v1.1` (they carry SimSpect's print-only hooks, which print only under the O3PipeView debug flag, which AMuLeT never enables, so they are inactive). AMuLeT's own YAMLs and `--extra-args` from `src/benchmark_all.sh` (SpecLFB `--SpecLFB`; CleanupSpec `--ruby,--CleanupSpec`; InvisiSpec `--ruby,--InvisiSpec,--InvisiSpec_Futuristic`), with only the gem5 paths and output dirs changed (`yaml.diff` in each run dir). AMuLeT's defaults: 200 programs per instance, `-i 70` (140 inputs per program after boosting), contract CT-SEQ; 32 parallel instances per defense (the paper used 100 on 128 cores), random generator seeds (AMuLeT's default, seed 0 = random). One change outside AMuLeT: the AE `configs/revizor/ipc.py` writes its assembled harness to `<gem5>/build/X86/`, which did not exist in the Ruby trees (CleanupSpec, InvisiSpec build to `build/X86_MESI_Two_Level`), so every gem5 died; I created the empty dirs `build/X86` there (no source or binary touched). The first CleanupSpec attempt was killed and is kept as `runs/FAILED_CleanupSpec_20261110T012934Z`.

**What one "test case" is (AMuLeT source).** A *program* is one generated test (one `fuzzer.py` round). AMuLeT's "test case" (its throughput unit) is program × input: `src/benchmark.py:230` counts `programs * input_count * 2` = 140 per program. A violation is one violating program (`fuzzing_round` returns at most one), after AMuLeT's priming validation. Programs to first violation = the `Progress:` count on the status line where `Viol:` first rises.

**Measurement.** `benchmark_t5.py` = AMuLeT's `benchmark.py` with exact, asserted replacements (`STAGE3_gem5/timing/amulet_t5/make_benchmark_t5.py`): the ISA-spec path, and measurement only: at each `Viol:` increase it records the program count, wall time and the CPU-s of that instance's process group (cli.py + its 4 gem5 IPC workers, from /proc); at exit it reaps each instance with `os.wait4`; at the end `RUSAGE_CHILDREN`. Per-instance CPU summed equals `RUSAGE_CHILDREN` (checked). First violations are classified by replaying them with AMuLeT's own analyser (`analyse_ipc_violation.py`, path-only copy) under debug flags, then: SpecLFB by the paper's UV6 signature (a squashed load SpecLFB flags speculative, `isCUSL` or `ismUSL` = 1, but `isUnsafe` = 0, brought the differing line in; p12 §4.7); CleanupSpec by AMuLeT's own `src/cleanupspec_analysis.py`, unchanged (UV3 = "Speculative Store Not Cleaned", UV4 = split, UV5 = too much cleaning, KV2 = I-cache only). Every replay reproduced its violation.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

From reading the code, AMuLeT's `benchmark.py` timeout path will crash before writing `info.txt` (it stores a float in `results`, `src/benchmark.py:188`), so use `t5_summary.json`, written by tmux window `sttfinal` |

- AMuLeT's own summary (`info.txt`), next to the paper's Table 4: SpecLFB avg. detection time 1.55 s (paper 1.6), throughput 2,347 test cases/s (paper 2,595), campaign 382 s (paper 18 min); CleanupSpec 2.81 s (paper 1.1), 1,302/s (paper 2,592), 688 s (paper 18 min); InvisiSpec 8.19 s (paper 2.1), 405/s (paper 630), 2,210 s (paper 1 h 14 min). Our runs used 32 instances, not 100, so the per-second rates are not directly comparable; CPU-s are. Every first-violation class matches the paper's finding for the unpatched build (InvisiSpec all UV1, p10; SpecLFB UV6, p12; CleanupSpec UV3 is the first bug it reports, p11), apart from the 9 SpecLFB cases noted in the table.
- InvisiSpec's first-violation distribution is uneven: 28 instances found UV1 within 32 programs, while 4 found nothing in 200. Each instance has its own random generator seed, so the mean over the 28 understates the expected cost; the pooled rate (23.7 programs, 270 CPU-s per violation) includes the 4.
- InvisiSpec vs SimSpect T1: SimSpect found no UV1 in 1,500 STT_6_fv2 tests (its tests never fill an L1D set, while AMuLeT pre-fills conflicting lines), so there is no SimSpect InvisiSpec number to compare against yet.
- Comparison with SimSpect T2/T3 (same machine, CPU-s): CleanupSpec, SimSpect UV4 first at test #47 (130 CPU-s), expected 89 tests / 241 CPU-s, vs AMuLeT median 17 programs (2,380 program×input runs) / 70 CPU-s, pooled 101 CPU-s per violation. But AMuLeT's first CleanupSpec violations are all UV3 (speculative store not cleaned, from its RMW/store-heavy programs), which STT_6_fv2 cannot reach, and SimSpect's are UV4: different bugs. SpecLFB, SimSpect UV6 at #47 (77 CPU-s) warm and #1 (3 CPU-s) cold vs AMuLeT median 23.5 programs (3,290 program×input) / 58 CPU-s, pooled 70 CPU-s per violation.

### SimSpect first detection, one fanout per defense (firstdet-simspect, 2026-11-09)

**Corpus (one for every defense, Anna 2026-11-09: "just get the rmws in with the regular run"):** `STT_6_fv2fp_rmw` = fv2fp (fanout-v2 vocabulary: inc, cmov, `br_cmp`, `jmp_direct` p 0.5, plus floating-point registers) with a deterministic 50% of load transmitters made value-neutral RMWs; built by firstdet-cleanupspec at cafe3 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/testsets_twostage/STT_6_fv2fp_rmw/` (READY file there when complete). Order: `timing_driver.py --seed 20261109` over the corpus's (mode, stem) pairs, the same procedure as the fv2 rows (`order.tsv` in each run dir; identical across rows).

**Configs (Anna 2026-11-09: AMuLeT's cache sizes, only our own cache setups, each run separately, no AMuLeT-style priming):** `runconfigs/amulet_parity_fv2fp_rmw/`: `T1` (InvisiSpec-Futuristic, warm; clflush setups are refused on InvisiSpec), `T3` (SpecLFB warm), `T3c` (SpecLFB cold), `T3r` (SpecLFB `random`, default seed `simspect-cache-random-v1`, each record carries the setup it used), `T4` (amulet-STT warm). Each is a path-only copy of the matching `runconfigs/amulet_parity_fv2/` file (results_name only; the model is `--model STT_6_fv2fp_rmw`), plus the one `cache_setup` key for T3c/T3r. An AMuLeT-mimicking `prime` setup (a copy of AMuLeT's per-test L1D thrash) was written and then withdrawn and reverted on Anna's instruction ("we have to do it our way"); `cache_setup.py` has no such strategy and is identical on the Mac and cafe3 (with firstdet-cleanupspec's two-barrier `find_barrier` fix; unit tests pass on cafe3, 19/19).

**Classification additions** (`STAGE3_gem5/timing/classify_hits.py --testset <corpus>`): (1) **H-PAD exclusion** (Anna's decision, URGENT_TODO "DECIDED 2026-11-09"): an event whose instruction PC is at or past the end of `.text` (the harness zero padding, decoded `add %al,(%rax)` on wrong-path fetch in mispredict_taken) does not count; a target signature carried only by such events becomes `excluded_HPAD:<sig>` and is reported separately. `.text` end = `objdump -h` of the test rebuilt warm, plus `cache_setup_info.delta` when cold rewrote it. (2) **STT `S1_xmm4`:** the transmitter's backward register slice touches `%xmm4` (`triage/diag/_fv2_slice.transmitter_slice`, the static half of `diag_stt_xmm4_skip.py`). The diag's GPR-twin half has no twin for this corpus; the parity baseline stands in for it: T4 on GPR-only STT_6_fv2 hit 0/1000. (3) **STT data-obtained gate:** a check_ld hit whose record has `data_obtained: false` is `disq_not_obtained(<cache_fate>)`, not a detection (CLAUDE.md "Leak criteria").

**Run:** cafe3 `gem5_dev`, shared with firstdet-cleanupspec (load is not dedicated, so CPU-s are cafe3 numbers, not comparable to cafe1's). Launchers `STAGE3_gem5/timing/firstdet_launch_T3_T4.sh` and `firstdet_launch_rows.sh` (T1, T3r) (copy at cafe3 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/results_fv2/firstdet/`; waits for READY, then T3, T3c, T4 with N=3000, 10 workers each, `TMPDIR=/tmp` for H-STK). Same cause as the fv2 T1 caveat: at 32 kB/8 with our warm start no test fills an L1D set, so a speculative load never has to evict. So on amulet-STT, check_ld's `issued_in_window` flags RMW transmitters that never executed: a check_ld false-positive pattern on RMW (`orq $0,(mem)`) transmitters, not counted; worth a look before any RMW campaign on STT builds (FLAG for Anna).

### AMuLeT per-bug first detection (amulet-perbug, 2026-11-09)

**What changed vs T5.** T5 classified only each instance's FIRST violation. Here every violation of the SpecLFB, CleanupSpec and InvisiSpec T5 runs (248 + 246 + 271 = 765 violation dirs) was replayed with AMuLeT's own analyser under debug flags (`classify_t5.py --all`, unchanged; 18 replays per defense in parallel on cafe1, 8 min 43 s wall) and classified with the same rules as T5. Every replay reproduced its violation (0 `not_reproduced`). Then, per instance and per bug, the first violation carrying that bug gives AMuLeT's tests to first detection, with T5's definitions: programs = the `Progress:` count when `Viol:` rises; program×input = programs × 140 (`src/benchmark.py:230`); CPU-s = that instance's process-group CPU at that moment (`t5_instances.json`). Mean / median / min are over the instances that found the bug; the others are censored at 200 programs and listed as "found k/32". The pooled rate (all 6,400 programs and all instance CPU-s of the campaign / violations carrying the bug) is the unbiased number when many instances are censored.

**Mapping violation → instance and program.** Round from `config_input1.ini` (or `config_input2.ini`: 5 SpecLFB dirs name the process only there; 1 SpecLFB dir names neither and was matched by time to the one round with a spare event 1 s after it). Within a round, the k-th dir by elapsed time is the k-th `Viol:` increase (asserted: dirs = events per round, and each dir's elapsed ≤ its event's wall time). A violation in an instance's LAST program never reaches a status line (the fuzzer exits first; 3 such cases: SpecLFB rounds 16 and 30, CleanupSpec round 25), so it gets the instance's final program count and CPU. So the true violation totals are 248 / 246 / 271, not the 246 / 245 / 270 that the logs' `Viol:` counts and the T5 table give.

**Classification.** CleanupSpec: AMuLeT's `src/cleanupspec_analysis.py`, unchanged; it reports ONE cause per violation in priority order UV3 (`is_rwm_bug`) > UV4 (`is_split_req`) > UV5 (`is_too_much_cleaning`), and no violation hit its I-cache path (KV2). As a sensitivity check, `cs_nonexclusive_t5.py` runs the same unchanged predicates on every violation without the priority: 2 UV3-labelled violations also satisfy UV4 and 1 also satisfies UV5. InvisiSpec: T5's heuristic (UV1 = each differing L1D line absent in one run is in the L1D set of a squashed load of that run, with an `L1_Replacement` in its ProtocolTrace; UV2 = AMuLeT's analyser reports a non-speculative violation). KV1 is an L1I effect and AMuLeT's InvisiSpec YAML traces `gem5_trace_mode: "data_cache"` (L1D + TLB), so KV1 cannot show up as a violation in this campaign. SpecLFB: T5's UV6 rule (paper p12 §4.7), plus the jmp-shadow signature below.

**SpecLFB jmp-shadow in AMuLeT's own violations** (`perbug_t5.py`, from the native `Branch`/`LSQUnit`/`Speclfb` debug output of the replay; same rule as `speclfb_analyze.py`'s `jmp_shadow` tag): a squashed load that brought in a differing line, never flagged speculative at any translation (`isCUSL` = `ismUSL` = 0), with a direct unconditional `jmp`/`call` (disassembly `jmp 0x…`/`call 0x…`) fetched on that load's path between the squashing branch and the load, and the squashing instruction itself not a direct jmp (it never is: all squashers are `jcc`/`loop*`). **47 of AMuLeT's 248 SpecLFB violations carry jmp-shadow (24 of 32 instances).** It is the sole explanation in 40 (all 40 that T5 called "squashed load never flagged speculative" apart from 2), and 7 more also have a UV6 load. Example: `config0results/0hrs-00mins-08secs`, reference run, `jge 0x4021bc` at 0x4021b8 (sn 10168) mispredicted, its fall-through `jmp 0x4021d7` at 0x4021ba (sn 10171), then the load at 0x4021ec (sn 10182) translated with `isCUSL: 0. ismUSL: 0 … isUnsafe: 0` and filled line 0x3ac0. This is exactly the README mechanism (an if/else join `jcc; jmp` in the shadow), and AMuLeT's generator emits that `jcc X; jmp Y` shape in every basic block. The earlier T5 note "3 follow a mispredicted direct jmp" was imprecise: the jmp is not what mispredicts; the older conditional branch is, and the jmp sits in its shadow. The other SpecLFB non-UV6 classes: 51 `unsafe_load_resent_after_LFB_stall` (7 of them have a direct jmp in the shadow, the analogue of our `replayed_after_safe(jmp_in_shadow)` tag; not counted as jmp-shadow), 2 never-flagged loads without a jmp, 5 unclassified.

The paper describes the fix as updating cleanup metadata in `Sequencer::writeCallback()` for speculative stores (p11, Listing 3). Running Patched would need a copy of the AE tree with that source change, a rebuild (X86_MESI_Two_Level), and another 32 × 200 campaign (CleanupSpec took 688 s wall here). That is a gem5 source change plus a new campaign, so not done (needs Anna's OK).
- Where (cafe1): `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/timing/amulet_t5/perbug/` = `perbug_replay.sh`, `perbug_t5.py`, `cs_nonexclusive_t5.py`, `logs/` (`perbug_replay.log`, `replay_<D>.log`, `perbug_t5.log`, `cs_nonexclusive.log`), `out/` (`perbug_summary.json` with every instance's per-bug first, `violations_<D>.json` with every violation's round/k/event/class and, for SpecLFB, the per-load jmp-shadow evidence, `cs_nonexclusive.json`). Each run dir also has `t5_classes_all.json`, and each violation dir has `analysis_t5/` + `analysis_t5.txt`. Scripts are also in the repo (`STAGE3_gem5/timing/amulet_t5/`). Two CleanupSpec replays (`0hrs-02mins-05secs`, `0hrs-09mins-27secs`) end with an AttributeError in AMuLeT's analyser's report printing (`SquashMemoryDependence` has no `vaddr`) after both gem5 runs finished; their cache tags and traces are complete and both classify as UV3.

### Cache setup `full` (cache-full, 2026-11-09)

**Why (Anna 2026-11-09):** InvisiSpec's UV1 needs a speculative load to evict a live L1D line, which happens only when the line's set is already full. At AMuLeT's 32 kB/8-way L1D our tests touch a few dozen lines, so no set ever fills, and T1 (warm) found no UV1 in 3000 tests; clflush is a no-op on InvisiSpec, so `cold` is refused there. AMuLeT pre-fills the L1D with lines that conflict with its sandbox; Anna rejected copying that and chose our own defense-agnostic setup, run as a 4th setup on all three defenses.

**What `full` is** (`STAGE3_gem5/cache_setup.py`, doc `wiki/design/SIMSPECT_HOOKS.md` "Cache setup strategies"): right after the prologue barrier, one plain `movq` per 64-byte line of a page-aligned `.bss` buffer exactly the L1D size (from `--l1d_size=32kB`: 512 loads), in address order, unrolled (no branch), `%rax` saved/restored, then `mfence`. It knows nothing about the test's addresses. PCs re-resolved and checked as for `cold`. Every record carries `cache_setup: full` + `cache_setup_info` (`fill_bytes` 32768, `n_loads` 512, `l1d_sets` 64, `set_cover_exact` true). Unit tests 28/28 on cafe3 gem5_dev (19 earlier + 9 new) and 28 (7 build tests skipped) on the Mac.

**Configs:** `runconfigs/amulet_parity_fv2fp_rmw/T1f_STT_6_fv2fp_rmw.jsonc` (= T1 + `cache_setup: full`), `T2f_STT_6_fv2fp_rmw.jsonc` (= `amulet_parity_fv2/T2c_STT_6_fv2fp_rmw.jsonc` with cold → full), `T3f_STT_6_fv2fp_rmw.jsonc` (= T3 + full); `results_name` is the only other change. `config.ini` of each run: L1D 32768/8 (parity).

**Smoke (20 tests per defense, `MODE=smoke`, every trace kept; `STAGE3_gem5/timing/full_smoke_check.py` replays the L1D fill/evict hook events of the prologue and setup block, which carry set and way):** 60/60 tests start with **all 64 sets holding 8 valid ways**; 0 errors; every annotation re-resolved. Test-region L1D fills that had to evict a line: CleanupSpec 5/5, SpecLFB 41/41. On InvisiSpec, 50 speculative-load (`specld`) evictions by test loads in 20 tests (49 by squashed loads), and 510 by the sweep's own loads (InvisiSpec-Futuristic issues them as speculative loads too; they commit). A first check counted 60-63 full sets; that was a replay-order bug in the checker (an evict and its replacing fill at the same tick), fixed, not a gap in the cache. A variant with an `mfence` before every load (as AMuLeT's thrash loop does) gave the same full state at twice the simulated time and was dropped. Other builds, 4 tests each, all `status: ok` with `cache_setup: full`: amulet-STT (T4 config), `/work/stt`, `/work/gem5-recon-modded` (cafe3, `cache_setup_fill_bytes: 32kB`, since their configs pass no `--l1d_size`), `/work/gem5-spt` and `/work/gem5-recon-ruby3` (cafe2 `bugwork/cachefull/`, scratch code copy; ruby3 with fill 64 kB = its L0D, because `full` refuses `--l0d_size` configs where `--l1d_size` is the second level).

**Classification addition (`classify_hits.py`, `full` records only):** with no lc instruction the window starts at tick 0, so the sweep's own InvisiSpec `specld` evictions looked like UV1 (smoke: they were in the offending events). Offending events whose actor PC is inside the setup block are removed before any rule runs; a signature carried only by them becomes `excluded_SETUP:<sig>`. Also new and reported next to UV1: `UV1_transient` (the evicting load never became visible, checker vp = inf). `classify_hits.py --jobs N` added (same per-hit function in N processes; T3f output byte-identical to the single-process run).

**Run:** cafe3 gem5_dev, only these runs, 3 rows × 16 workers (load about 48/64, so CPU-s are higher than the dedicated cafe1 numbers), `TMPDIR=/tmp`, launcher `STAGE3_gem5/timing/firstdet_full_launch.sh`, seed-20261109 order (same as every other row). 0 errors in all three.

### SimSpect vs AMuLeT, tests to first detection: the comparison (hooks-v2, 2026-11-09)

Combines the three subsections above: SimSpect = "SimSpect first detection, one fanout per defense" and "CleanupSpec first detection with RMW fanout" (corpus `STT_6_fv2fp_rmw`, AMuLeT's cache sizes, only SimSpect's own cache setups, cafe3 shared, so SimSpect CPU-s are upper bounds); AMuLeT = "T5: AMuLeT on cafe1" (AMuLeT's own fuzzer, YAMLs and seeds, 32 instances × 200 programs × 140 inputs, cafe1 dedicated). SimSpect "tests" = one program run once; AMuLeT reports programs and program×input runs (`src/benchmark.py:230`). SimSpect expected tests = (N+1)/(K+1) over a random order; AMuLeT = mean over instances of programs to its first violation. AMuLeT starts CleanupSpec and SpecLFB from a flushed sandbox (p9; `configs/revizor/ipc_base_x86.s:49-57`), so SimSpect's `cold` is the like-for-like setup there.

**AMuLeT column updated per bug (amulet-perbug, 2026-11-09):** the AMuLeT numbers below are now per bug (the first violation carrying THAT bug in each instance; "AMuLeT per-bug first detection" above), not each instance's first violation of any kind. Means are over the instances that found the bug; "k/32" says how many did; the pooled rate (6,400 programs / violations with the bug) is given where most instances are censored.

Ratios on the like-for-like pairs (SimSpect cold vs AMuLeT mean over finders; AMuLeT's mean is biased low wherever instances are censored): SpecLFB UV6 ≈35× fewer programs, ≈5,000× fewer simulated runs, ≈22× less CPU; SpecLFB jmp-shadow ≈34× fewer programs (≈51× against AMuLeT's pooled rate), ≈4,800× fewer runs, ≈23× less CPU; CleanupSpec UV3 ≈0.6× (AMuLeT needs 1.7× fewer programs), ≈83× fewer runs, ≈0.5× CPU (cafe3 loaded, so SimSpect CPU is an upper bound); CleanupSpec UV4 ≈1.4× fewer programs than AMuLeT's mean over its 4 finders but ≈28× fewer against its pooled rate (1,600 programs per UV4 violation), ≈200× fewer runs, ≈1.3× less CPU (≈22× pooled); UV5: AMuLeT finds it (4/32, pooled 1,600 programs), SimSpect does not. Open: which unit the abstract uses (URGENT_TODO), whether the per-bug AMuLeT number is the mean over finders or the pooled rate (URGENT_TODO DECIDE, amulet-perbug), and how to frame jmp-shadow now that AMuLeT's own runs trigger it (URGENT_TODO DECIDE, amulet-perbug).

### SimSpect vs AMuLeT, CPU time per detection, per bug (hooks-v2, 2026-11-09)

Same estimator on both sides: total CPU-s ÷ detections. SimSpect = (N+1)/(K+1) × CPU-s per test from the firstdet rows on `STT_6_fv2fp_rmw` (cafe3 shared, load about 50/64, so SimSpect CPU-s are upper bounds; on dedicated cafe1 the earlier per-test cost was about 1.6-2x lower). AMuLeT = pooled CPU-s per violation of that bug from "AMuLeT per-bug first detection" (cafe1 dedicated, whole fuzzer). SimSpect "total" = build + gem5 + checker; "gem5" = gem5 only.

| bug | SimSpect random: total (gem5) CPU-s | SimSpect cold: total (gem5) | AMuLeT pooled CPU-s | multiple, random total | multiple, cold total |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| UV6 (SpecLFB) | 6.1 (1.7) | 4.3 (1.1) | 115 | 19x | 27x |
| UV3 (CleanupSpec) | 293 (111) | 167 (57) | 105 | 0.36x (AMuLeT 2.8x faster) | 0.63x |
| UV4 (CleanupSpec) | 288 (109) | 280 (95) | 6,171 | 21x | 22x |
| UV1 (InvisiSpec) | `full` setup (random/cold not possible): 7.3 (1.4) | — | 270 | 37x (`full`) | — |
| UV5 (CleanupSpec) | pending (model fix) | — | 6,171 | — | — |
| jmp shadow (ours) | 16 (4.5) | 11 (2.8) | 366 | 23x | 34x |

Its UV3/UV4/UV5 counts are still informative.

Then `make_rmw_corpus.py --select eligible --fraction 0.5 --seed 20261109 --complete`: eligible 4,787 / 4,789 of 5,461 ld tests per mode, **4,788 RMW tests** (`STT_6flat_fv2fp_rmw`); then `make_jmp_corpus.py --p 0.5 --seed 20261109`: 11,918 tests with an inserted jmp. **UV5 precondition survived** (`uv5_timing_scan.py`, all 12,000 not_taken): committed load whose address needs a producer **257 (2.1%)**, same line as a squashed load with U's address ready later than S's **211 (1.8%)**, ≥3 later 108 (0.9%); old flat STT_6 had 1.7% / 1.6% / 0.85%, and the two-stage corpus 0 / 0.

## Two-stage model families (Stage 1 core → Stage 1.5 flesh-out)

**What a core file is:** not a separate model. Each `_core` file is the production model's defense block, byte-identical, plus only the Stage-1 minimality and the scope relaxation (`but 6` instead of `exactly 6`, idx symmetry-breaking removed), made by `proposed_twophase/derive_core.py`. All core files are **unapproved drafts**, including the `STT_6_core.als` prototype in `models/` (committed 2026-10-20, never signed off). Anna signs off clause by clause before any production campaign or paper number.

**Minimality (decided 2026-11-08, Anna): RI-only for every family, SPT included.** SPT can use RI-only now because SPT_6_oneLP_v2 contains the seed fix. RO+RI makes the STL cores impossible. The STT_6/Recon_6 core drafts are still RO+RI and must be re-derived; the owner is Anna's Alloy agent, and since these are `.als` drafts, per-change sign-off applies.

**Stage 1.5:**
- **Not built for any family.** Anna's Alloy agent owns the two-stage ladder: `stage_seed.py` / `stage_pipeline.py` are an unwired STT prototype, and making them parallel is the open TODO.

**If SPT's Stage 1.5 volume is unwieldy** (its RI-only Stage 1 caps at 5,000 already at `but 4`), a scope ladder (enumerate N = 3..6 and keep the smallest distinct shapes) is the smaller but still conservative alternative.
