**SPT_6_oneLP_v2: what changed** (resolve-hooks; checked by simspect-c8 against `SPT_6_oneLP.als`: exactly these four edits, each marked `[v2-n]`, everything else byte-identical).

| | change | why | effect |
|---|---|---|---|
| v2-1 | add `rf_from_most_recent_writer`, verbatim from `STT_6.als:161-170` | only STT_6 had it; in 68% of the old SPT testset an rf edge reads a register past a newer writer, so parsexml's program is not the instance | removes only instances no register machine can realize |
| v2-2 | protection seed computed over the perturbed program: `o_p[p] - ((Inreg + Inaddr) - i_p[p].outreg.(rf_p[p]))`, and `[p]` at the call site | the old seed ignored perturbations, so removal minimality could never shrink it and the SPT core was empty | identical to the old seed when nothing is perturbed (scratch `eq_base`: 0 counterexamples), and a subset under every perturbation. So it changes no leak verdict and loses no instance; it adds instances (≥500 capped in production) and makes SPT cores satisfiable. It is **not** the A1 seed rewrite in `ALLOY_MODEL_CHANGES.md` |
| v2-3 | base clause `not secure_speculation_scheme_p[no_p]` (≥1 insecure transmitter) instead of `one_insecure_…` (exactly 1) | the skeleton form STT/Recon use; SPT alone pruned two-leaker tests | strictly adds instances; `xm` still names the one the checker scores |
| v2-4 | declassification removes only the transmitted value and its producer (`.*(~rf_p)`), not the whole rf+ddi backward cone | SPT untaints backward only through MOV, and through invertible arithmetic when all but one input is public (paper §6 "Untaint rules"; gem5-spt `rob_impl.hh:853-878`) | the protection set becomes a superset of SPT's (more tests); some hits will be SPT legitimately untainting through mov/add, which the x86 slicer classifies |

**Not included, on purpose** (Anna prefers too many tests): P3, which drops constant-derived transmitters, and P5, the skeleton-wide unfed-read fact. Both are in `ALLOY_MODEL_CHANGES.md`.

**Evidence** (`results/spt_v2_20261108/`):
- v2 is satisfiable: 3,000 instances in 2 min at `exactly 6`.
- Shape census, old → v2: stale rf 63% → 0, clean 23% → 58%, unfed read 28% → 33%, constant-derived transmitter 2.4% → 9.6%.
- Differential: ≥500 instances in both, and ≥500 gained by v2.

**"Lost" checks** (old-useful and not new-useful):
- For v2 as a whole: only v2-4 can lose an instance, through minimality. Both runs are inconclusive: the 90-min Mac run and the 8 h cafe2 run (`/tests/simspect_fixed_20261030/triage/spt_v2/lost_cafe2.log`, finished 2026-11-09 08:22 UTC) timed out with no counterexample, and the cafe2 one never got past CNF translation (resolve-hooks, `wiki/findings/audits/2026-11-08/SPT_V2_AND_HIT_TRIAGE.md` §6.3).
- For v2-2 alone: simspect-c8's Mac run **timed out after 4 h with no counterexample**. That is not a proof, since it never reached UNSAT; the proof is the argument (equal at no perturbation, a subset under every perturbation, monotone propagation), which resolve-hooks audited independently (`results/twophase_stage1_2026-11-03/probe_spt_seed_20261107/differential_20261108/run.log`).
- That run's log labels the model "sha 65eae944". That is the SHA-1 of the same unchanged file whose SHA-256 is 713d0904, not a different file.

## STL (store-to-load speculation): builds, per-defense scope, experiments

**What STL is here** (`STL_SPECULATION.md`):
- An older store's address is held unresolved, and the memory-dependence predictor is forced to say "no alias".
- A younger load to the same address reads the stale value. When the store's address resolves, gem5 squashes the load (memory-order violation).
- The window runs from load issue to that squash (Spectre v4).
- The opposite direction (predict alias when the addresses differ) opens no window and is only a negative control.

**Builds:**
- **2026-11-16: `/work/gem5-recon-modded-stl`** (cafe3 tree, binary also on cafe1; md5 `06240ccea173d11b3b2be61adcdbe387`) = today's `gem5-recon-modded` (resolve hooks included) + the STL hooks. Use it for every new ReCon / Recon-STT STL run (row E2w-stl below).

**STL experiments done:**
- **Ultimate STT_6_u_stl two-stage, 1% (2026-11-09 18:43Z, DRAFT; cafe2 `results_stl_u/`, the stl-campaign scorer unchanged):** 488 stems (353 ld, 135 br_x; 48,017 deduped corpus, atom-based alias model). In-window ld transmitters: Unsafe 343/353, STT 0/353, NDA 0/353, DoM = Unsafe (warm-cache L1 hits; DoM criterion DECIDE). Unlike STT_6_stl (NDA 8/467, the R-SA-18 store-forward shape), the atom-alias model's corpus produces no NDA in-window transmitter in this sample.
- Hook test, 60 `STT_6_stl` tests on `gem5-recon-stl` (2026-10-30): 58/60 valid bypasses. STT sent 0/28 load transmitters in the window vs Unsafe 28/28; the `alias` and hold-0 controls gave 0 (`results/STT_6_stl_hooktest/controls/`).
- Shadows store-only + alias (2026-11-07): STT 0/32 vs Unsafe 32/32 (`results/STT_6_shadows_draft/`).
- **1% campaign on the two-stage STT_6_stl family (stl-campaign, 2026-11-09; draft: no STL window checker, stale build).** 717 sampled stems (`sample_keep(stem, 0.01)` over the 65,398 compiled `stl_bypass` stems; 467 ld + 250 br_x transmitters) on `gem5-recon-stl`, hold 5000, `no_alias`, warm caches, scored by a path-only copy of `stl_hooktest.py` (ld transmitters only). Results, logs and notes: cafe2 `/tests/simspect_alloy_20261108/results_stl/` (`RUN_NOTES.md`, `summary.json`).
  - Valid bypass: Unsafe 707/717, STT 707, NDA 694, DoM 707 (the invalid ones fail only the 16-byte `squash_ok` case; NDA also has 14 where L did not execute before release).
  - ld transmitter sent in [hold, release): **E1 Unsafe 453/467, E2 STT 0/467, E8 NDA 8/467, E9 DoM 453/467.** Agrees with the 2026-10-30 hook test (STT 0/28 vs Unsafe 28/28).
  - DoM equals Unsafe on every timing field of all 717 records. This is not a leak signal: DoM sends speculative loads as L1-only packets (`/work/gem5-recon-stl/src/cpu/o3/lsq.cc:1443-1446`) and delays only misses, and with warm caches every xmit hits L1. A DoM STL run needs cold or random cache setup plus a hit/miss-aware criterion.
  - NDA (scheme 1, `Delay` in `cpu.hh`) candidate leaks, 8 stems, timings identical to Unsafe: inst-005328, inst-005911, inst-006285, inst-020279, inst-021314, inst-021955, inst-030235, inst-063118 (in inst-020279 the xmit's address register comes straight from the bypassing load). **Root-caused 2026-11-09 (bug-investigate-ndastl): all 8 are R-SA-18.** In each, the bypassing load is store-forwarded from an older resolved store, and the forward path skips NDA's delay. The 451 stems where L goes to memory send 0 (SOURCE_AUDIT_RECON.md R-SA-18 'gem5 check').
  - br_x transmitters (250) are not scored by the smoke-test scorer.

## Big corpus bug coverage (2026-11-15)

Anna (2026-11-15): "make sure that the big corpus can find [all our bugs] ... make sure that the race is on spt and stt to be sure." One pipeline, the final recipe of the overnight 2026-11-11 plan (fv4 table, RMW + jmp as Stage-2 vocabulary via `fanout_v2.vocab_rj`, interleave-only fillers, no post-passes): corpus **A** = fillers only (the coverage claim), corpus **B** = A + chain interleaving (new recipe, reported separately). One big corpus per defense family from the production two-stage path (`twostage_lp.py --alloy-only`, Stage 1 cores reused, Stage 1.5 target 60,000 distinct, budget 9,000 s, shuffle seed 20261115): `Footprint_6_u_core_lpP_s2{A,B}` (InvisiSpec, CleanupSpec, SpecLFB; the 1,411 regen-lp cores), `STT_6_u_core_lpP_s2{A,B}` (STT, Recon-STT, race), `Recon_6_u_core_lpP_s2{A,B}` (ReCon). How it runs, where, and how to resume: `wiki/state/RUNNING.md` "Big corpus 2026-11-15". Configs `runconfigs/bigcorpus_20261115/` (copies; headers list every changed key).

**Rows.** Footprint (Anna 2026-11-15: the SimSpect side of the AMuLeT comparison is rerun on this corpus, so the 2026-11-10 firstdet-final protocol is replicated exactly): the parity configs T1g / T2g / T3g (copies of `runconfigs/firstdet_final_20261110/`, only paths and corpus changed), random_all cache setup, the 3x3 timing grid, `TMPDIR=/tmp`, all transmitter kinds, `firstdet_stream.py` (reusing `timing_driver.run_one`, every per-record CPU and timing field kept) with seed 20261109; CleanupSpec T2g over the whole corpus, InvisiSpec T1g and SpecLFB T3g the first 4,000 of the seed order (`--seed-prefix 4000`); classifiers `classify_hits.py --incremental` (which runs `cleanupspec_skip_evidence.py` and `speclfb_analyze.py`), `classify_cs_rmw.py`, `firstdet_report.py`, with the same exclusions (H-PAD, setup-block events under full, UV3/E→M only on the test's own RMW). GPR twin (recipe A with only fpr off; `scripts/bigcorpus_20261115/e2w_bucket.sh`) of every program with a gated plain hit: 12,266 faithful tests (370 dropped for a jmp filler; the twin's vocab_rj equalled the corpus test's in every case). recon_stlf evidence on 4,933 ld keys. **UV3 (R-SA-8, ReCon oldest taint): 24 candidate tests (13 not_taken, 11 taken; 13 programs), 22 of them carry the R-SA-8 signature in a faithful rerun with Commit,DelayUnit,Rename (`rsa8_trace.py`: transmitter branch released from the delay unit before the window branch's held resolve fires; inst-000685 in both modes does not).** Static check of the mov false dependency (`scripts/bigcorpus_20261115/movmerge_check.py`, straight-line register slice of the transmitter's condition, approximate): 19 of the 24 have a reg-reg `mov` whose old destination descends from an older load, i.e. they get the older operand through gem5's x86 mov false dependency (E2LT-2); the 3 signature-positive tests without it are inst-003908 (both modes) and not_taken inst-013804. **UV2 (A2, ReCon STLF bypass): 1,690 tests** (plain with twin evidence 598 + 597, plain main-run evidence 16 + 15, RMW main-run evidence 235 + 229). Other buckets: R-SA-13 (SSE-load producer) dominant; `isTainted=0 (reveal not checked)` 540 + 535 plain ld (the reveal check of the regen-lp method was not run); check_ld RMW artifact 373 + 372; 1 + 1 untainted RMW transmitters without a twin. **Correction (2026-11-16, fig8-otb-v5; `paper_figures/otb_v5/README.md`):** inst-003908 does use gem5's false dependencies (its merge is the `mov rax, rax, rsi` micro-op; the old rax carries i3's taint through gem5's `cqto` micro-op reading rdx), which `movmerge_check.py` misses because it ignores implicit operands, so at least 21 of the 22 use them. `rsa8_trace.py` does not check that the released branch is the transmitter: in 5 of the 22 keys (inst-000366 and inst-008487 in both modes, not_taken inst-013804) the transmitter never enters the delay unit (possible shape B, unproven). ### Why A-UV5 is not found (uv5why, 2026-11-16)

Anna asked "why didn't we hit uv5" and then (2026-11-16, via the coordinator) "figure out what is different from last time we did this and why we dont find it", which approves a comparison with the 2026-11-10 run (corpus `Footprint_6_u_core_lpP_fv3`, `stage2_fv3.jsonc` + RMW post-pass at 0.5 + jmp post-pass, run `firstdet_final/T2g_*_merged`: UV5 in 4 tests = 2 programs, inst-021207 and inst-007645, both modes) for this diagnosis only. Diagnosis only; nothing in the pipeline was changed. Work dir cafe3 `/tests/bigcorpus_20261115/uv5why/` (census outputs `{old,new}_nt2*.out`, per-stem dumps `*_nt2.dump`, rerun dirs `run_uv5new/`, `run_uv5sld/` with all traces); census script `STAGE3_gem5/triage/uv5_shape/uv5_census_cmp.py` (a copy of `uv5_timing_scan.py` that also reads the `load_swapidx` form `(%data,%lea)`, which the original scan mis-reads as "unknown address", and tallies the S/U forms per pair).

**Answer.** The UV5 shape is just as common in the unified corpus; what changed is the two programs that found it. Both drew an RMW (`orq $0, mem`, `vocab_rj`) for the squashed load S, and CleanupSpec never cleans up after a squashed RMW: its access goes out as a store (`req=st`, GETX) and the cleanup is skipped (`L1D:cleanup_skip … why=nohitinfo`). With no `cleanup_inv` there is no over-cleaning. When only S is turned back into a plain load, 3 of the 4 tests hit UV5 again under the same cache setup and timing point. UV5 is rare (2 programs in 25,116 on 2026-11-10), so losing those two programs to the RMW draw is enough to explain 0/50,232. There is no sign of a systematic loss.

- **(A) Same programs, both runs.** Each test drew the same setup in both runs (the draw is keyed by mode and stem): inst-021207 cold, timing (10000, 2500), stall `{3: 10000}`; inst-007645 cold, (2500, 0), `{3: 2500}`. On 2026-11-16 all four tests ended `hit=False` with `terms_fired=[]`; on 2026-11-10 all four were `over_clean`. U is unchanged in both programs: an FP-routed `ldfp` (021207 pc2 `movq (%rdx,%rsi), %xmm4`; 007645 pc2 `movq (%r13,%rsi), %xmm8`), indexed by an earlier load's value, with the same `pxor/por` route on pc0. S changed. In 021207, pc5 (the transmitter) was `movq (%rdx,%rsi), %rsi` on 11-10 and is now `orq $0, (%rdx,%rsi)`. In 007645, pc4 (a non-transmitter load) was `movq (%r13,%r12), %r9` with an FP route and is now `orq $0, (%r13,%r12)`; the transmitter pc7 is also an RMW now. The rest also differs, but none of it is needed to explain the loss. Fillers: 11-10 used ALU fillers (`imul`, `and`); today they are interleave-only (`movq %r8,%xmm14; pxor; movq`, `bts/bsf`, `sete`). There is no `jmp` splice today (11-10 had `jmpins` in 021207 not_taken and 007645). The branch form changed in 021207 not_taken (`cmpq $1; jae` → `testq; jne`). One store has the form `movq %r14, (,%rdx)`.
- **(C) Re-run under the exact 11-10 draw.** Today's code and config (`T2g_Footprint_6_u_nov10_s2A.jsonc`, with only the paths changed), `firstdet_stream.py --only-file --keep-all-traces`. `uv5new` = today's asm verbatim: 0/4. `uv5sld` = today's asm with only S's RMW replaced by the same plain load (`movq …` + `nop`, so every address stays the same): **3/4 `over_clean`** (021207 both modes, 007645 not_taken). Trace for 021207 not_taken. With the RMW: S seq 3190 at 17403000 `L1D:miss … req=st msg=ST ev=Store` and `L2:miss … GETX`; U seq 3131 hits 0x75c0 at 17491500 (st=M); then at 22584000 `L1D:cleanup_skip:line=0x75c0:seq=3190 … why=nohitinfo`, and the trace has no cleanup_inv for that line. With a plain load: S seq 3189 misses (GETS) and fills E at 17490000; U hits at 17491000; at 22583000 `cleanup_req`, then at 22583500 `L1D:cleanup_inv … ev=Inv_CLEANUP st=E`, then at 22597500 `L2:cleanup_inv`. That is the UV5 chain of 11-10. The 4th test (007645 taken) still misses with a plain S, because U now runs first. U (seq 3184) issues at 17411500 and misses; it fills 0x75c0 at 17481500. S (seq 3199) issues at 17482000 and hits, so its cleanup is `cleanup_skip why=l1hit`. On 11-10, S issued at 17425000 and U at 17496500, after S's fill. The pc0 load that U depends on returns at 17406501 today and returned at 17453501 on 11-10. The concretization around it differs: different fillers before pc1, a different divisor register, and no committed-path `jmp`. Which of those changes reverses the order was not pinned down.
- **(B) Static census, both corpora, not_taken, all 25,116 programs.** Old → new. Same-line pairs (U committed, S after the first unresolved branch, same static line): 10,849 → 10,551. U's address ready later than S's (class D): 690 → 566. Of those, with a plain-load S: 566 → 443. Same-line tests with at least one plain-load S: 7,774 → 8,308. The S forms of all squashed loads are ld 31,162 / rmw 10,294 on 11-10 and ld 30,553 / rmw 10,532 today, so about 25% RMW in both. U forms: 21,695 gpr / 11,900 xmm on 11-10, 18,155 / 11,671 today. Counting a committed RMW as U: 21,919 / 11,671. So the shape and the RMW share are about the same. The only static drop is about 20% in class D. The first pass, with the unmodified scan, showed a 38% drop (6,673 same-line). That drop was entirely the `load_swapidx` artifact: the new tables emit `(%r11,%rsi)` with the lea register as the index. The taken-mode census is not meaningful for the 11-10 corpus, because the scan stops at `retq` and the taken layout puts the speculative block after it (10 tests with any squashed load).
- **How likely 0 is by chance.** 11-10 found UV5 in 2 programs. With the shape kept at about 80-97%, the expected count today is about 1.6-1.9 programs, so P(0) is about 15-20% (rough Poisson, 2 events). The two programs that carried it today both drew an RMW S, which explains this run's 0.
- **What would have to differ for UV5 to be reachable.** In this build, UV5 needs S to be a plain (GETS) load, so that CleanupSpec cleans it up, plus the dynamic order "S fills before U issues". The unified recipe gives about 25% of squashed loads an RMW, the same share as 11-10, so it does not block UV5; it just lowers the odds per program. Raising the UV5 rate would mean steering the RMW draw away from loads that share a line with an older committed load. That is tuning toward one bug, which CLAUDE.md forbids unless Anna decides it.

## Resolved-branch gate (2026-11-16)

Anna (2026-11-16) chose option (b), gate on late resolved branches, and then RG-1 = (b), correct path only. **Rule:** every resolved (`correctly_not_taken`) branch with spo after lc, before the transmitter, AND older than the oldest unresolved (mispredicted window) branch must be visibly resolved strictly before the transmitter's signal, else the record is not a hit. Wrong-path resolved branches never gate (reported in `record.resolved_gate.wrong_path`); a test without a window branch (store shadows) has all its older uncommitted resolved branches on the correct path. Code: `STAGE3_gem5/resolved_gate.py` (pure function), `gem5_common.process_one` (criteria key `resolved_older_before_signal`; NOT deployed to any node: inttwin and the random-cache ReCon reruns were live), tests `STAGE3_gem5/tests/test_resolved_gate.py` (9 OK); design `wiki/design/RESOLUTION_HOOKS.md` §3; commands `wiki/state/COMMANDS.md` "Resolved-branch gate re-score". Offline re-score from the saved raw records, no gem5: run dir `experiments/2026-11-16_resolved_gate/` (extracts by `run_extracts{,2,3}.sh`, read-only on the nodes; scoring by `rescore_all.sh`; log `jobs.log`; `scores/<campaign>.json`, `scores/<campaign>_removed.jsonl`, `scores/instance_diag.txt`; the superseded all-paths scoring is kept in `scores_v1_allpaths/`). Removed hits are joined to each row's existing per-record labels (`labels/`); classification is per record, so classify.py was not rerun.

**Per campaign (hits = issued_in_window records; every saved record had `resolved_branches`; spo and address order agree in every test).**

| campaign | records | hits before → after | removed | removed by label |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| E2w ReCon-STT (36 shards, cafe3) | 30,368 | 18,029 → 18,029 | 0 | (no hit has a correct-path uncommitted resolved branch) |
| E2w-stl ReCon store shadows (20 shards, cafe1) | 60,342 | 33,671 → 33,047 | **624** | R-SA-13 430 (incl. static 134, FP-elsewhere 3), check_ld RMW artifact 88, isTainted=0 35, never_executed 12, **A2/UV2 58**, **R-SA-8/UV3 confirmed 1** (inst-008363) |
| UV1 S1 check xmm4 / xmm3 / GPR (/work/stt) | 496 each | 448 → 448 / 0 / 0 | 0 | — |
| SPT-Fence sptrace d1 / d4, 2,000/mode | 4,000 each | 738 → 738 / 509 → 509 | 0 | — |
| SPT-Fence sptrace merged d1 / d3 / d4 | 9,928 each | unchanged | 0 | — |
| race lines SPT 24 stems d1 / d4; STT inst-047231 (stt, stt-master; d1/d3/d4) | 24 / 24; 1 each | unchanged (spt24 d4: 3 correct-path branches, all resolved in time) | 0 | — |
| Footprint T1g, T3g, T2g main / extra-new / c1 / c2 / c3 | hits only | unchanged | 0 | every uncommitted older resolved branch in these corpora is on the wrong path, so the gate never applies (RG-2 moot for them) |

UV6 race metrics (race_scan, gate at the access tick): unchanged everywhere (sptrace d4 not_taken 1,674 / 2,000, taken 1,250; d1 0; 40/40 confirmed stems). inst-047231 keeps its delay-3/4 transmission (its late branch i4 is wrong-path).

**E2w-stl removals in detail.** All 624 removed hits fail on a correct-path vocab/filler branch that becomes visible more than 2 cycles after the signal; **no same-tick tie** and no branch that never resolved. 474 have a swept delay (500 or 2,500 cycles) on a failing branch; 150 fail with 0 added delay (the branch resolves late without help). Instance sensitivity (RG-3, `_resolution_of` takes the youngest instance): 80 of the 624 have an older instance of a failing branch visible before the signal, none of them UV2 or UV3.

**Bugs affected.** UV2 on store shadows 2,907 → 2,849 (bucket_v2; the paper's pre-triage 2,906 → 2,848; −2.0%). UV3 on store shadows 61 → 60 confirmed (not a paper number). Every other bug, row and paper count: unchanged.

**Paper numbers (ISCA-27 `sections/results.tex`, read-only), old → new:** UV1 "98 leak" (of 100) → 98, unchanged; UV2 "1,690 of the 30,368" → 1,690, unchanged; **UV2 "2,906 of the 60,342" → 2,848**; UV3 "22 of the 30,368" → 22 and "19 of these 22" → 19, unchanged; UV6 "1,674 of 2,000", "none of 2,000", "all 40" → unchanged; the inst-047231 example → unchanged; Figs. `firstdet_*`, `timedet_*` and every A-UV / UV4 / UV5 count → unchanged.

**Committed-branch cross-check.** Every hit with a signal: 0 exceptions. Footprint (checked against lc_retire): 1, T1g taken inst-023003, an instance-choice artifact (the committed instance seq 1604 is visible at 18,883,000 < lc_retire 18,883,500; `_resolution_of` records a younger re-fetched instance that never resolved).

## Decisions

**Decided (Anna, 2026-11-08):**
- Scheme 1 = NDA.
- Regenerate every testset once the two-stage model exists; everything is NOT RUN.
- Stage-1 minimality: RI-only for every family.
- STL this round: the Kaxiras-group builds (rebuild `gem5-recon-stl`, write the STL checker).
- SPT seed fix (now inside v2).
- Alloy runs on the cafe2 container.
- Footprint rows run shrunk caches; AMuLeT parity is only for timing and bug confirmation (P-1).
- CleanupSpec TBEs: 8 for the eval, 256 for parity, never 2.
- amulet-STT glue fixed, so E4 is unblocked (P-4).
- STT roles: E3 = SimSpect's STT results; E4 = AMuLeT comparison.
- RMW as a disjoint corpus (C1).
- Comparisons use AMuLeT's flags as the reference.

**Also decided (Anna, 2026-11-08, later):** SPT_6_oneLP_v2 is the SPT model; it stays in `models/`; delete the briefs once their content is here; start every Alloy run now, at 6 instructions; grow the vocabulary before the fanout runs.

**Open:**
- **Models:**
  - (SPT v2 approved; the cafe2 "lost" check on v2-4 finished inconclusive: 8 h timeout, no counterexample. A check that finishes is proposed in `wiki/state/OPEN.md`);
  - P3 (proposed: no);
  - P5, the unfed-read fact for all models;
  - give `Recon_6.als` the rf fact;
  - approve each core draft clause by clause, and re-derive the STT_6/Recon_6 cores RI-only;
  - NDA / DoM / Doppelganger defense blocks.
- **Pipeline:**
  - build and parallelize Stage 1.5, then regenerate;
  - set `sample_fraction` for the final batch;
  - `make sync` before the final batch.
- **Checkers:**
  - a criterion for store transmitters (E11 `str`);
  - the InvisiSpec D-1 bucket;
  - the SpecLFB walk-order rule;
  - whether jmp-shadow counts in the paper, and contacting the authors (C3).
- **Configs:**
  - P-2 (InvisiSpec needsTSO/allowSpecBuffHit for the eval rows);
  - P-3 (keep E12b, Spectre);
  - P-5 (SpecLFB predictor for the eval row);
  - the eval rows' clock (they run gem5's 1 GHz default; parity runs 2 GHz);
  - needsTSO / Ruby for E3 and E11.
  - **ReCon (recon-ruby, 2026-11-08):** `se_ruby.py` approved (#11) and the authors' cache sizes kept (#12). Still open: (c) a ReCon stimulus that can exercise the reveal (a committed pair, then a speculative load that issues after LD2 commits), because Recon_6 never does. The old classic runs are relabelled Recon-STT (#16).
  - **stt-master:** the pending-squash mirror in the resolve-stall hook is decided and done (#13; see "stt-master").
- **STL:**
  - who rebuilds `gem5-recon-stl` and writes the checker, and the checker's design;
  - approve `Recon_6_stl`;
  - SPT STL's RS rule (exempt the alias location / drop RS / skip);
  - `rf` vs atom alias fact;
  - `STT_6_shadows`;
  - whether the forced memory-dependence prediction counts as "the predictor".
- **Other:**
  - how OTB tests are produced (E7);
  - whether gem5-spt has an STT sub-mode (E5);
  - the Doppelganger training stimulus (E10);
  - C2.

## Later: AMuLeT on the defenses it never tested

This is Anna's urgent STATUS todo of 2026-11-08, planned for after the eval.

**The goal:** run AMuLeT itself, with its own parameters, on every SimSpect defense AMuLeT never tested: E1, E2, E3, E6, E8, E9, E10, E11, and the STL build.

**What each needs:**
- AMuLeT's µarch-trace extraction ported into that build (check whether our v2 hooks can stand in for it);
- the contract AMuLeT would use for that defense (ARCH-SEQ for STT-like defenses, CT-SEQ for footprint-like ones; AMuLeT §3.1 p5);
- a parity config taken from that build's own authors, where AMuLeT has none.

**Status:** not planned; write the plan before any run.
